## What this is
A macOS-style dock for Windows 11 in C++20 / Win32 + Direct2D + DirectComposition; one vendored dependency since round 4 phase 4, the QuickJS-ng script engine for widgets (`third_party/quickjs-ng`, MIT).
Full design: `C:\Users\olson\.claude\plans\need-you-to-come-async-grove.md` (copy it into the repo as `PLAN.md` if that path is gone).

## Decisions made by the user (keep these)
- Renderer default stays `"auto"` (hardware D3D11, WARP fallback). `"software"` is available in config.json for ~8 MB private bytes instead of ~65 MB; the difference is the NVIDIA user-mode driver.
- No working-set trimming hacks (no `SetProcessWorkingSetSize`). Report real numbers.
- Taskbar fully hidden while running; dock floats and auto-hides (macOS behaviour, Phase 5).
- Efficiency over features; no large dependencies.

## Build and run
```
cmd /c '"C:\Program Files\Microsoft Visual Studio\18\Community\VC\Auxiliary\Build\vcvars64.bat" && cd /d E:\repos\windows-dock && cmake --preset x64-release && cmake --build --preset x64-release'
E:\repos\windows-dock\build\x64-release\WinDock.exe
E:\repos\windows-dock\build\x64-release\WinDock.exe --quit
E:\repos\windows-dock\build\x64-release\WinDock.exe --restore-taskbar
```
Config: `%LocalAppData%\WinDock\config.json`. Crash marker: `%LocalAppData%\WinDock\taskbar-hidden`.

## If the taskbar is missing
The dock hides it. Run `WinDock.exe --restore-taskbar`, or simply launch and quit the dock. If that fails, restart Explorer: `taskkill /f /im explorer.exe && start explorer`.

## Testing cautions learned
- Do not use Notepad as a test app on this machine; it restores unsaved tabs and pops a save dialog.
- `explorer.exe C:\` opens tabs in the existing window on this build, so it cannot be used for a two-window test. Use two `mspaint.exe` windows.
- Listary's `SearchBarWindow` appears next to file dialogs; the tracker ignores `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE` windows without `WS_EX_APPWINDOW` for this reason.
- Always finish a test run with `--quit` and confirm `Shell_TrayWnd` is visible and no `WinDock` process remains.
- Element model (1b): a widget slot is ONE plain visual in `widgetsGroup_` (content drawn into `item.layer`, placed with OffsetX / OffsetY, like the old `clockLayer_` / `weatherLayer_`), not the icon slot's transform group + five children. Elements hold no visuals and are rebuilt (`SyncElements` / `BuildElements`) whenever the item list changes; the slots keep their visuals across a rebuild, matched by key (widgets: `kind:clock`, `kind:clock#1`, `kind:weather`). The item list is only rebuilt once the tracker has started (`tracking_`): at Create the first snapshot builds it right after, so an earlier rebuild (pins without their windows) is wasted work.
- Hidden test messages (registered window messages, all documented in README.md): `WinDock.QueryItemPoint`, `WinDock.ItemCommand` (800 pin toggle, 801 new window / bin Open, 802 file location, 803 folder in Explorer, 804 Choose icon dialog, 805 reset icon, 806 folder popup toggle, 807 empty bin, 808 Choose image dialog, 900 close all), `WinDock.DockCommand` (100 auto-hide toggle, 101 hide taskbar, 102 autostart, 103 open config, 104 show taskbar until re-enabled, 105 quit, 106 reload config, 107 settings window, 108/109/110 auto-hide off/always/intelligent, 111 refresh weather, 112 add separator, 113 weather settings, 114 calendar popup toggle, 115 weather popup toggle, 116 lock dock toggle, 117 / 118 / 119 add widget clock / weather / running apps, 120 / 121 / 122 / 123 edge bottom / top / left / right; 112 and 117-119 do nothing while locked), `WinDock.QueryState` (0 auto-hide state, 1 notification state, 2 hovered item, 3 label visible, 4 magnifying, 5 pending timers, 7 drag state, 9 insertion index, 11/12/13 preview popup visible / cards / card window, 14 auto-hide mode, 15 overlapped, 17 reduce motion, 19/21 foreground-watch counters, 20 theme resolve failed, 22/23 folder popup visible / entries, 24 item kind (0 app, 1 folder, 2 separator, 3 Recycle Bin, 4 clock, 5 weather), 25 item custom icon, 26 drop mode, 27 weather state (0 none, 1 stale, 2 fresh, 3 error), 28 temperature x 10 (`INT_MIN` none), 29 weather popup visible, 30 seconds since the weather fetch (-1 none), 31 calendar popup visible, 32 icon effects applied (bit 0 glow, 1 reflection, 2 perspective), 33 glow opacity target x 100, 34 reflection surface height px, 35 item dominant colour, 36 locked, 37 index in `docks[0].items` of the config item behind slot lp, 38 edge (0 bottom, 1 top, 2 left, 3 right), 39 align (0 center, 1 start, 2 end), 40 offset (DIPs), 41 full width, 42 reserveSpace, 43 app bar registered, 44 peek px, 45 title drawn, 46 dock drag (0 none, 1 along the edge, 2 over another edge's zone), later ones in README (218 a script widget's passive popup visible, the volume bar); 24/25/33/34/35/37 take the item index in `lParam`). Since 1b item indexes count every slot in dock order, the clock and weather widgets included (one slot each): with the default order `[pins..., running, weather, clock]` the pin / running indexes are unchanged (the widgets come last, the clock at the end); scripts that took the widget position from "last item + 58 px" can now ask `QueryItemPoint` for the widget's own index (its box centre). `QueryState` 2 never reports a widget (no hover label / preview over it), `ItemCommand` on a widget index is a no-op.
- QueryState 5 does not count launch bounces. To check that a click did not bounce, watch the dock HWND's height: a bounce keeps it at stage size (128 px at 100 %) for up to 5 s, otherwise it compacts to the bar (64 px) about 200 ms after the mouse leaves.
- Test configs must keep `"startWithWindows": true` (the user's setting): Reload config (DockCommand 106) applies it to the Run key.
- With `autoHide` `"off"`, a window that is maximized and activated (two `mspaint.exe` windows open maximized here) can end up above the topmost dock, so hover tests miss the dock. Restore and shrink the test windows first (`ShowWindow(SW_RESTORE)` + `MoveWindow`). Seen while testing 8.0; z-order code was not changed, so this is most likely an existing gap of the "off" mode (only hide transitions re-assert topmost).
- The machine may be in use during a test run (during the 8.0 run the pins were reordered and the cursor moved without the test script doing it); re-read the item list before asserting on positions or order.
- Folder / Recycle Bin click tests open Explorer views in the user's Explorer window (tabs); leave them, closing a tab window via `Shell.Application` can close the user's other tabs.
- PowerShell P/Invoke: pass `[NullString]::Value` (not `$null`) as the title to `FindWindowW`; `$null` binds as "" and matches only untitled windows. Build from PowerShell: in Git Bash `cmd /c` gets its `/c` path-mangled.
- Click popups (the folder popup; later weather / calendar) are activatable: opened by a real click on the dock they take the foreground, so Escape and clicking another window close them. Opened by `WinDock.ItemCommand` 806 they usually cannot take the foreground (the dock did not receive the input): Escape does not reach them, but another window coming to the foreground, a dock click or 806 again closes them. Before clicking "into" a popup in a script, check `QueryState` 22 is 1: a dock click on an open folder's icon toggles it closed, and the click then lands in whatever window is behind (twice during 8.1 testing it landed in the user's Chrome window).
- Recycle Bin tests: recycle only a temp file of your own (`Microsoft.VisualBasic.FileIO.FileSystem::DeleteFile(..., SendToRecycleBin)` or a scripted drop), then take it back out with `Shell.Application` (`Namespace(10)`, find the item by name, `MoveHere` it to a temp folder, delete it). Never run Empty Recycle Bin (`ItemCommand` 807) unless the bin holds only your own files; during 8.1 it held ~200 of the user's items, so Empty and the full -> empty icon switch were not run.
- Scripted OLE drags work from PowerShell: a small topmost WinForms form as the source, `mouse_event` left-down, then `Application.DoEvents()` a few times BEFORE `DoDragDrop` (the drag loop reads the thread's synchronous key state; without that the drag ends at once), and a second process moving the cursor (`SetCursorPos` + small real moves) and releasing. The source form is itself a new window on the dock (an extra running item re-centres the dock by half a slot), so locate items after it appears. Scripts: session scratchpad `p1\drag.ps1`, `p1\mover.ps1`.
- Do not use `IApplicationActivationManager::ActivateForFile` on packaged desktop apps (Paint): it fails with "the app does not support the contract" and was seen to end Paint's running instances (unsaved work). The drop code uses the registered `IAssocHandler` instead.
- Driving the item menu: open it with a real right-click (SafeClick-style check that WinDock is under the pointer), then find the menu window (`#32768`), get its `HMENU` with `MN_GETHMENU` (0x01E1) and press Down until `GetMenuState(id)` has `MF_HILITE`, then Enter. Send keys only while `GetForegroundWindow()` belongs to WinDock. Scripts: session scratchpad `p2\menu.ps1`, `p2\common.ps1`, `p2\t2.ps1` / `t7.ps1` / `t8.ps1`.
- Settings buttons that open a modal dialog (Choose icon…, Add…) must be pressed with `PostMessage(WM_COMMAND)`, not `SendMessage` (the call would block until the dialog closes). A dialog of WinDock's is the visible `#32770` of the WinDock process; close it with `PostMessage(WM_COMMAND, IDCANCEL)` when the foreground is not WinDock's. `GetWindowText` on another process's edit control returns nothing (use `WM_GETTEXT`).
- A modal dialog owned by the dock disables it: the dock then ignores clicks, and the dock must not re-assert `HWND_TOPMOST` (see 8.2). Any new dock-owned modal dialog should run through the same hold + CBT focus fix as `App::ChooseItemIcon`.
- Weather tests need the network (api.open-meteo.com, geocoding-api.open-meteo.com). Testing the widget writes `%LocalAppData%\WinDock\weather-cache.json`; delete it afterwards unless the user's config has `weather.show` on. The weather timer (id 20) is not an interaction timer: `QueryState` 5 stays 0 while it waits. To see the Stale state, restart the dock with a cache for the same place and units: `QueryState` 27 is 1 until the first fetch of the session completes (well under a second here), then 2. The widget's screen position is not queryable: take it from the last item's `QueryItemPoint` (+58 px at 100 %, clock about +125 px with the date shown) or from a screenshot, and check with SafeClick that WinDock is under the point. Take positions from the rest layout: `DockRect` while the mouse is on the dock is the whole stage, not the bar. Popups opened by `DockCommand` 114 / 115 can usually not take the foreground (as with `ItemCommand` 806); to test "another window comes to the foreground" without clicking another app, start a small WinForms window in a separate process and bring it forward with an Alt keypress + `SetForegroundWindow` (scratchpad `p3\form.ps1`, `p3\t2.ps1`).
- The Bash tool in this environment unescapes backslashes in command text (heredocs included): write files that contain backslashes (C++ escapes, JSON paths) with the Write tool, not `cat <<EOF` or inline Python.
- Building from the Bash tool: the documented `cmd /c '"...vcvars64.bat" && ...'` line silently does nothing there (Git Bash mangles `/c`; `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` then breaks the quoting). Write a small `.cmd` (`call vcvars64.bat`, `cmake --preset`, `cmake --build`) into the scratchpad and run it from PowerShell: `& cmd /c <path>\build.cmd`.
- Before / after comparisons: build the previous commit in a scratchpad `git worktree add --detach <dir> HEAD` (its own `build\x64-release`), run it on the same config, and compare `QueryItemPoint` x per index plus `QueryState` 24: the bar is centred, so the widths of the clock / weather show up as shifted x values (the dock rect is useless while intelligent auto-hide keeps it hidden). Remove the worktree afterwards.
- Settings pages are created eagerly, so their controls can be driven while hidden: find them by id under `WinDock.Settings` with `EnumChildWindows` + `GetDlgCtrlID`, then `BM_SETCHECK` + `WM_COMMAND(id, BN_CLICKED)` to the control's parent; `LVM_GETITEMCOUNT` reads the Dock contents list (scratchpad `settings.ps1`; selecting rows: see the cross-process list view caution).
- A save drops theme keys whose value equals the preset's (existing override logic): the user's `"reflection": false` / `"perspective": false` under the `light` preset disappeared on the migration save. Expected, not a migration bug.
- Pixel comparisons between builds: DirectComposition sub-allocates surfaces from atlases and D2D's high-quality cubic `DrawBitmap` (also on WARP) depends on where in the atlas an icon surface sits, so icon edge pixels can differ by 1-3 levels between two runs with a different surface allocation history (measured: d0713d3 started normally vs d0713d3 started without widgets and then given them by Reload config: 369 pixels, max delta 2, icon edges only). Everything else (background, hairlines, widgets, positions, magnified states) compares exactly. Compare rest screenshots with a tolerance, or magnified ones (the magnified-size icon surfaces compared identical). Two runs of the same build are bit-identical apart from the clock.
- PowerShell test scripts: variable names are case-insensitive, so a dot-sourced helper that sets `$script:Exe` overwrites the caller's `-Exe` parameter, and `$a = ...` in a script with a `[string]$A` parameter converts the value to a string. `Move` is an alias of `Move-Item`. A process started with `-WindowStyle Hidden` also hides the first form it shows (STARTUPINFO), so a scripted drag source must not be started hidden, and it must check that its own form is under the cursor before pressing (scratchpad `dragsrc.ps1`, `t9.ps1`; the mover waits for the source's "dragging" log line).
- Test configs with `"autoHide": "off"` keep the dock shown for screenshots; the user's own config is `"intelligent"` (the dock is usually hidden while a window overlaps it, `QueryState` 0 = 2).
- 1c test scripts: session scratchpad `c1\` (`h.ps1` helpers on top of `common.ps1`; `t-compare.ps1` 8f63bfd vs new slot points, `t-a12.ps1` / `t-a2c.ps1` seconds and clock target, `t-a345.ps1` / `t-a4n.ps1` previews, middle click, wheel on Paint, `t-b.ps1` / `t-bshot.ps1` / `t-glitch.ps1` widget drags, `t-c.ps1` lock, menu, Add widget, OLE drop, `t-d.ps1` Dock contents page, `t-e.ps1` General / Behaviour controls and pin drags vs 8f63bfd). Test configs come from `config.off.json` with a PowerShell mutation (`WriteCfg`), which keeps `"startWithWindows": true`.
- Cross-process list views (the Dock contents list 2600): select a row with `LVM_SETITEMSTATE` and read cells with `LVM_GETITEMTEXTW` through an `LVITEMW` written into the dock process (`OpenProcess` + `VirtualAllocEx` + `WriteProcessMemory`; x64 offsets: state 12, stateMask 16, pszText 24, cchTextMax 32), `C1::SelectRow` / `C1::RowText` in `c1\h.ps1`. This covers 1a's "not exercised" Move up / down / Remove.
- A popup menu opened by `TrackPopupMenuEx` in the dock process (the Dock contents page's Add widget ▾, the dock menu) can be driven without the foreground: find its `#32768` window, `MN_GETHMENU`, then post `WM_KEYDOWN` VK_DOWN until `GetMenuState(id) & MF_HILITE`, VK_RETURN (or VK_ESCAPE). `GetMenuState` by command id gives MF_CHECKED / MF_GRAYED for Lock dock (116) / Add separator (112); the Add widget submenu is found by position (`MF_BYPOSITION`, flag `MF_POPUP` 0x10).
- PowerShell `$null` passed to any C# `string` parameter arrives as `""` (not only `FindWindowW`): use `[NullString]::Value`. Hex literals like `0xFFFFFFFF` are `int32` -1 in PowerShell: for `mouse_event` wheel data pass `[uint32](4294967296 + $delta)` for a negative delta.
- `calc.exe` is a stub that starts the packaged Calculator: a launch by the dock shows up as a short-lived `calc` process (within ~50 ms), `CalculatorApp` a few seconds later on a cold start. Match `*calc*` and kill both afterwards (only when none ran before the test).
- Widget drags: press on the widget's `QueryItemPoint`, glide in ~24 real moves, read `QueryState` 7 / 9 before releasing. Once, the very first scripted drag after a dock start did not start (no capture, nothing moved); re-running passed every time since. Escape during a drag: `keybd_event(VK_ESCAPE)` (the dock polls `GetAsyncKeyState`).
- Phase 2 test scripts: session scratchpad `p2\` (`h.ps1` on top of `c1\h.ps1`: `MakeCfg` / `SetDockKey` mutate `config.off.json`, `Shot`, `Compare-Png`, `MoveTo`; `t-cmp.ps1` 76cd3f2 vs new, `t-full.ps1 -Edge -Align -Offset` the per-edge battery, `t-edges.ps1` screenshots per edge, `t-settings2a.ps1`). The 76cd3f2 build lives in the scratchpad worktree `wt-base` while testing.
- Relative `mouse_event` moves go through pointer acceleration: a `+1` / `-1` nudge can leave the cursor one pixel off (seen as a 1 px magnification difference between two builds that was really a different cursor position). End every scripted move with `SetCursorPos` to the exact point plus a zero `mouse_event` move (`p2\h.ps1` `MoveTo`).
- PowerShell: `@($a.X, $a.Y - 12)` is `@(($a.X, $a.Y) - 12)` (the comma binds tighter than `-`): parenthesise arithmetic inside array literals.
- Settings edit boxes commit on focus loss: send `WM_COMMAND(id, EN_KILLFOCUS)` to the page after `WM_SETTEXT` (an Enter `WM_KEYDOWN` sent to the edit does nothing; the window handles Enter in its message loop).
- Scripted Ctrl + drags: never press Escape while Ctrl is down (Ctrl+Esc opens Start, which then eats the next clicks); release Ctrl first and make an own test window the foreground before sending Escape (it goes to the foreground window). Phase 2b scripts: `p2\t-2b.ps1` (full width per edge, reserve space with SPI_GETWORKAREA, peek, title, reflection clip), `p2\t-dockdrag.ps1`, `p2\t-settings2b.ps1`.
- Reserve-space tests: check `SPI_GETWORKAREA` before starting (taskbar shown: 0,0,5120,1392 here), while reserved (bottom 0,0,5120,1376), and after quit (back to the value before). With the Explorer taskbar shown the shell stacks it and the dock's band (1328). A hard kill (`Stop-Process -Force`) of a reserving dock is cleaned up by the shell itself (work area back to the full monitor; the crash marker restores the taskbar on the next start).
- On this machine a foreground window usually overlaps a bottom dock already (the user's maximized windows): intelligent-mode checks that need "no overlap" have to create their own foreground window first.
- Multiple docks (3a): send automation to the app window `WinDock.App` (always present) or any dock window; the dock is `HIWORD(lParam)` for QueryState / QueryItemPoint / DockCommand / ItemCommand (dock 0 = old scheme; `p3\h.ps1` `QSd` / `QPd` / `DockCmdD` / `ItemCmdD` / `ItemsD`). `FindWindow('WinDock.DockWindow')` returns any dock: get a specific dock's window from QueryState 51. Popup windows are unowned and one class per kind: with two docks pick the one beyond the right dock's bar (`p3\t-multi.ps1` `PopupRectA`). QueryState 47 count, 48 monitor index, 49 parked, 50 config index.
- Desktop layer (3b): `docks[].layer` `"desktop"` docks are hidden while any non-WinDock window is the foreground; to show one in a test, make Progman the foreground (Alt keypress + `SetForegroundWindow(FindWindow("Progman"))`, `p3\t-desktop.ps1`) instead of Win+D / `ToggleDesktop` (those minimize the user's windows). The user's maximized windows still cover it then: check its pixels with `PrintWindow(PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT)` of the dock window, not a screenshot. QueryState 52 layer, 53 shown.
- Settings > Layout canvas (2900): read the docks' canvas rects from its window text (`index:cfg:monitor:l,t,r,b;`), map with `ClientToScreen`, and check `WindowFromPoint` is the canvas before pressing (the settings window must be in front: Alt + `SetForegroundWindow`). Drags apply on release (`p3\t-canvas.ps1`).
- Reserve space now applies to every dock with `autoHide` `"off"` (not only full width), and the default is on: a test config with `"autoHide": "off"` shrinks the work area and resizes the user's maximized windows. Test configs set `"reserveSpace": false` unless the test is about it (`p3\h.ps1` `MakeCfg2`). The user's own dock (autoHide off, reserveSpace true) reserves 72 px at the bottom since 3a.
- PowerShell: a scriptblock passed to a function whose own parameter has the same name as a variable it uses (`$Mutate` inside a `MakeCfg2 { ... & $Mutate }` block) calls itself until the depth limit: during 3a this wrote a config with thousands of folder pins. Name helper parameters distinctly (`-Extra`).
- PowerShell `Get-Content -Raw` + `.Replace()` on repo files: they are CRLF, here-strings are LF, so multi-line replacements silently do nothing; use the Edit tool (or `` `r`n ``) for multi-line changes. Scratchpad scripts written by the Write tool are LF.
- PowerShell: never put `$(` inside a double-quoted regex (`"^    $(?=...)"` is a subexpression, the pattern degrades to `^    ` and strips indentation from every line). Use single quotes for regexes.
- Script widgets (phase 4): test widgets go in `%LocalAppData%\WinDock\widgets\<id>\` (the user has no such folder: delete the whole `widgets` folder after testing, junctions included: `t-errors.ps1` makes `t-sandbox\link` a junction to `widgets\outside`). `QueryState` 54 state of slot lp (0 ok, 1 error, 2 suspended), 55 / 56 widget count of the dock / of every dock, 57 heap bytes, 58 draw count, 59 scheduler wake-ups (process), 60 log lines, 61 / 62 a slot's box px along / across; `DockCommand` 126 reloads every widget, `ItemCommand` 809 one slot. Idle checks: QueryState 58 (draws) and 59 (wake-ups) must not move; the user's own config has `clock.showSeconds` on (the native clock ticks every second), so idle tests turn it off. A widget's state, not its log text, is what scripts can read in 4a: make a test widget throw in `draw()` to report a failed check (`t-errors.ps1` sandbox widget), or draw counters and read a screenshot (`t-events.ps1`).
- The user's dock runs from `build\x64-release`, so build work in progress in a separate tree (`build\dev`, scratchpad `p4\build-dev.cmd`: same compiler and flags) and only rebuild the preset at the end; the single-instance mutex means a test dock can only run while the user's is quit (`--quit`, restart it from `build\x64-release` afterwards with the user's config restored).
- Bash tool: a long `python - <<'EOF'` heredoc failed with "unexpected EOF while looking for matching `''" (the tool's own quoting); use the Edit tool (or a script file written with the Write tool) for multi-line source edits.
- Widget tests 4b (scratchpad `p4\`: `w4b.ps1` the test widgets, `console.ps1` Console helpers, `t-4b-*.ps1`): widgets report into their storage files (`%LocalAppData%\WinDock\widget-data\<id>.json`, written 1 s after a change and on quit), which a test reads back (`WData`; `Data` is a reserved word in PowerShell). Delete `widget-data` with the `widgets` folder after testing. Starting Calculator (`shell.open('calc.exe')`) disturbs the dock for seconds: the packaged CalculatorApp appears 2 - 5 s after the stub, takes the foreground (closing any click popup) and while it starts the dock's posted messages and timers ran late (sent QueryStates still answered; most likely the existing icon loading of a starting packaged app, not investigated): run shell.open checks last, in a session of their own, wait for `CalculatorApp`, then kill every `*calc*` process the test started. The first scripted click after a dock start was lost once more (as in 1c); a widget's slot index moves when a test window becomes a running app (re-read `WidgetSlots` after the drag source form appears).
- The generated Widget settings page (10) and every other page share the title id 2000: find a page's own controls under its page window (`[C1]::FindById((Ctl 210), 2000)`).
- Python heredocs run through the Bash tool get their backslashes unescaped first: `"widgets\\faces"` in a replacement became `widgets` + form feed + `aces` in README during 5a. Do backslash-bearing edits with the Edit tool or a .py written by the Write tool (scratchpad `p5\fixff.py` checks for control characters).
- PowerShell `{ ... }.GetNewClosure()` runs the block in a new module scope: functions dot-sourced into the script (`AddItem`) are not found. Pass data through `$script:` variables instead (`p5\t-5a-*.ps1`).
- 5a test scripts: session scratchpad `p5\` (`h.ps1` on top of `p4\h.ps1`: `BoxRect`, `Montage`, `WData`, `RestoreUserCfg`; `t-5a-faces.ps1`, `t-5a-timing.ps1`, `t-5a-rm.ps1`, `t-5a-settings.ps1`, screenshots in `p5\out\`). The user's config backup for round 4 phase 5 is `p5\config.user.bak.json`.
- The dock's UI thread is busy for ~0.8 s right after a start (icons): widget timers run late then; a startup animation must measure from its first frame (the analog clock's sweep does).
- Phase 6 tests (session scratchpad `p6\`: `h.ps1` on top of `p5\h.ps1` with `StartPaints` (refuses when mspaint already runs), `StartForm` (a WinForms window in its own process via `p3\form.ps1`), `SetTitle` (`WM_SETTEXT` from the script: the window's caption changes and the shell sends `HSHELL_REDRAW`, so this is the scripted "rename"), `SlotOf` (QueryState 73), `t-cmp.ps1` base vs new, `t-6a.ps1`; the 12241cc build in the scratchpad worktree `p6\wt-base`). The user's Chrome / Code / Terminal windows get window slots on an unstacked test dock: never hover or click those slots (their previews would show the user's windows); act on the mspaint and form slots only, found by handle. The user's config backup for phase 6 is `p6\config.user.bak.json`. Thumbnail tests must never show the user's windows: a config with a `running` item captures every window on the machine (the first 6b smoke run did; its screenshots were deleted), so 6b tests pin only Paint and the test exe `p6\wdtest\wdtest.exe` (csc-compiled WinForms: `wdtest.exe <title> <x> <y> <w> <h> <seconds> <anim 0/1>`) and have no `running` item. Live capture started from an automation `SendMessage` fails (`RPC_E_CANTCALLOUT_ININPUTSYNCCALL`) unless deferred: the dock posts it (QueryState 86 shows a capture start error).
- Audio tests (5b): record the user's state first (`p5\audio-orig.json`: volume 0.6, output MUTED, mic unmuted, on 2026-11-02) and restore it in a `finally` with the independent helper `p5\audio.ps1` (`AudioSnap` / `AudioRestore`, compares the float exactly). The volume keys unmute the output. After the volume keys were used, Windows applies / reports a mute change ~750 ms late: wait 1.5 s before reading a mute back. Never switch the default output on this machine; `setDevice` was only tested with the current default (all three roles were the same device).
- Media tests (5b): the user's Chrome usually has a (paused) YouTube session that is current: only read it, send commands only when the current session's app is Media Player (`Microsoft.ZuneMusic...`). Opening the WAV: `Start-Process file.wav` shows an "Open with" dialog (kill `OpenWith`), `MediaPlayer.exe <file>` opens Media Player with its last queue (the user's files), only `IApplicationActivationManager::ActivateForFile` with the ZuneMusic AUMID (`p5\activate.ps1`; Media Player must not be running) plays the generated silent file (`p5\media\windock-media-test.wav`). Media Player's window can cover the dock (minimize it: `WM_SYSCOMMAND SC_MINIMIZE`) and its running-app slot shifts the widget slots (re-read `WidgetSlots`). Kill only Media Player processes the test started.
- Start / Search / notification centre tests: on this build the Start menu's foreground window is SearchHost's `Search` CoreWindow (the same as a real Win key press); close each UI with Escape only while it is the foreground (`ForegroundInfo` in `p5\h.ps1`). Do not keep screenshots of them (they show the user's recent files and notifications). 5b scripts: `p5\t-5b-audio.ps1`, `t-5b-media.ps1`, `t-5b-shell.ps1`, `t-5b-mem.ps1`, test widgets in `p5\w5b.ps1`.
- Start menu host (round 16): the menu's window (`Windows.UI.Core.CoreWindow` "Start" of `StartMenuExperienceHost.exe`, `0,0 - 5120,1392` here) keeps any `SetWindowPos` shift while closed, so the next Windows key opens the menu shifted: after any test that may move it, check its rect (`Get-StartHostWindow` + `Get-WindowRect` in the module) and move it back first if it is off. `EnumWindows` (the module's `ByClass`) does not list the shell's CoreWindows; `FindWindowEx` does. Its UI Automation rectangles are empty while it is closed (cloaked).
- Phase 7 tests (session scratchpad `p7\`: `h.ps1` on top of `p6\h.ps1` with `Cfg7` (the user's config with a given item list, reserveSpace / previews / seconds off), `FolderItem`, `PopState`, `PopCmd` (ItemCommand 814 - 816), `PopupKey` / `PopupSysKey` / `PopupChar` / `PopupWheel` (posted to `WinDockFolder`), `DragFiles` (scripted OLE drag with `p7\dragsrc.ps1`); test folders under `p7\data` (f12, f500, fnest, fdrop, src); the 8ce0d03 build in the worktree `p7\wt-base`). **File drags must never be released over a window that is not WinDock's**: twice during 7a a scripted drag released over the user's VS Code window (once after a script error left the button down, once because the popup had closed before the release) and VS Code took the dropped scratchpad text file. `DragFiles` now releases only when `OnDock` is true at the target, otherwise back on its own source form, and releases in a `finally`. A click popup opened by `ItemCommand` 806 before the drag source presses on its form loses the foreground to it and closes: open the popup after the drag has started (`DragFiles -During`); the source form closing after the drop hands the foreground to another window, which closes the popup too (re-open it to check its contents).
- Phase 8 tray (session scratchpad `p8\`; spike `p8\spike\spike.cpp`, `run1.ps1`, findings in ROADMAP Phase 8): a second `Shell_TrayWnd` (the spike, or a test dock's TrayHost) takes every app's notify icon on the machine for as long as it lives (~35 real apps here re-register within ~1 s of the `TaskbarCreated` broadcast); every message is mirrored to Explorer, so Explorer keeps every icon and quitting / killing the host loses nothing, but always end a run by destroying the window and broadcasting `TaskbarCreated` (`--quit` does it) and check with `Trays` (`p8\w.ps1`) that only Explorer's `Shell_TrayWnd` remains. Run the spike only while the user's dock is quit (its TaskbarController looked for `Shell_TrayWnd` by class). PowerShell: `H` (Get-History) and `AC` (Add-Content) are aliases, a script function with that name never runs. Real apps' icons: observe only; never click into their menus beyond open + Escape (close a menu by posting `WM_KEYDOWN` VK_ESCAPE to its `#32768` window, never by a key press that could reach the user's foreground window). Logitech G HUB sends a `NIM_MODIFY` every second (with identical data): a tray dock wakes once a second because of it (not our polling). 8a scripts: `p8\h8.ps1` (helpers; `StartDock` waits ~3.4 s: the takeover is posted, and Explorer's raise right after it repeats the broadcast 1.5 s later), `t-8a.ps1` / `t-8a2.ps1` / `t-8a3.ps1` / `t-real.ps1`; the test app `p8\traytest\traytest.exe <log> <seconds> [extra]` with `TrayTest.Cmd` commands; `p8\dbwin.ps1` captures the dock's `OutputDebugString` lines (DBWIN buffer) for debugging; `p8\appbar.ps1` registers a third-party app bar (PowerShell: a nested struct field such as `$d.rc.L = 0` changes a copy, assign the whole `RECT`). Counting broadcasts in the test app's log: expect two after a start (takeover + the repeat).
- Phase 9 notifications (session scratchpad `p9\`: `h9.ps1` on top of `p8\h8.ps1` with `Cfg9`, `StartDock9 <filter> <denied>` (sets `WINDOCK_NOTIFY_FILTER` / `WINDOCK_NOTIFY_DENIED` for the dock process: keep the filter on our test AUMIDs, e.g. `windock`, so dismiss / dismissAll never touch the user's notifications), `Toast` / `Untoast` / `UntoastAll` (`toast.ps1`, Windows PowerShell 5.1, `SuppressPopup`, group `windock`, under the registered test AUMID `WinDock.Test.Notify`: `HKCU\Software\Classes\AppUserModelId\WinDock.Test.Notify`, remove it with `toast.ps1 unregister` when done), `TestToasts` (our "WD9" titles via the spike harness), the test app `ntest\ntest.exe <aumid|-> <title> x y w h secs` (with an AUMID its process and window carry it; `NTEST_LOG` gets "activated" lines), the test widget `ntw\` (copied into `%LocalAppData%\WinDock\widgets` only for a run). Never print the harness's full list: it contains the user's own notification texts (print counts or our "WD9" lines only). The session locks itself while tests run (`LockApp` in the foreground): screenshots then show a frozen screen and nothing can take the foreground; use `PrintWindow(PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT)` of the dock window (`pw.ps1`) for pixels, and leave magnification / activation by real clicks for an unlocked session. The user's own Chrome / FanControl toasts are read in some runs (read only).
- Phase 11 tests (session scratchpad `p11\`: `h11.ps1` on top of `p9\h9.ps1` + `p9\pw.ps1` with `Cfg11 <pins> <edge> <dockExtra> <tail> -icon N` (pins = `p11\pins\pinNN.lnk` from `mkpins.ps1`: pin00 Paint, pin01 Calculator, the rest distinct System32 programs that are never started; the monitor is 5120 px wide, so a bottom dock needs ~90 icons at 48 DIP to overflow: the bottom tests use 96 DIP icons), `SendMouse` / `SendWheel` (mouse messages SENT to the dock window with the cursor put there too: while the session is locked real input does not reach the dock, `WindowFromPoint` answers the lock screen, but sent messages and `GetCursorPos` work), `MagnifyAt` (ItemCommand 834), `QP11` (signed QueryItemPoint: h8's `QP` throws on a slot left of the monitor), `DiffPng` (pixel diff via LockBits; `Add-Type` with System.Drawing types does not compile under PowerShell 7, so the C# part takes byte arrays); the d415dde build in the worktree `p11\wt-base` (`build-base.cmd`). A sent `WM_MOUSEMOVE` arms leave tracking and, with no real pointer on the window, a `WM_MOUSELEAVE` follows at once: hover / magnification cannot be driven by sent moves, hence ItemCommand 834. The scroll-drag and icon-drag paths ignore `WM_MOUSELEAVE` while they own the mouse. Ring tests: `t-11b.ps1 -Shape -Edge -Count` (positions, magnification, label, popup, click, drag) and `t-11b2.ps1` (auto-hide, Ctrl + drag, scrolling, rotateIcons, Settings); an auto-hidden dock cannot be revealed by a sent move either (the leave that follows kills the reveal delay), so they show it with DockCommand 108. The user's config backup for phase 11 is `p11\config.user.bak.json` (it had `reserveSpace` false, `thumbnails` snapshots, `titles` one and `tray.enabled` true on 2026-11-03).
- Phase 13 tests (session scratchpad `p13\`: `h13.ps1` on top of `p11\h11.ps1` with `Cfg13 <feeds> <edge> <items> <calExtra>` (running apps + clock and `calendars.feeds` from `FeedJson name url color refresh enabled`), `StartDock13`, `CalSelect` (ItemCommand 835), `CalWnd`, `CalSize`; the test feed `feeds\work.ics` (copied to `work-copy.ics` for each run, which the file test edits) and `expected.ps1` (`ExpectedDays`: its per-day counts computed independently); the user's config backup `p13\config.user.bak.json`). Web feed tests use Mozilla's holiday calendar (ETag) and a 404 URL there; they write `%LocalAppData%\WinDock\calendar-cache` and must delete it afterwards (the user has none; `t-13a-web.ps1` refuses to run when it exists). Never click an event row that has a URL (`work.ics`'s Standup has one): it opens the user's browser. In the locked session a freshly shown popup is often captured black by PrintWindow until it commits again (seen for the calendar popup and the reminder banner, also on a dock without feeds, so not a phase 13 regression): send it a key or a mouse move (a hot change redraws) before capturing (`t-banner-dbg.ps1`). 13b: test widgets in `%LocalAppData%\WinDock\widgets` and their `widget-data` must be deleted afterwards (`t-13b.ps1` refuses to run when either exists). Bash heredocs unescape `\n` inside Python string literals too: edits with backslashes go through the Edit tool or a .py written by the Write tool.
- Phase 12 folder windows (session scratchpad `p12\`: `h12.ps1` on top of `p11\h11.ps1` with `Cfg12 <folder entries>` (a clock dock + `FwJson id path x y w h extra` entries), `StartDock12`, `FQ` / `FCmd` (QueryState / ItemCommand for a folder window's dock index), `FRect`, `FWnd`, `FHashes` (QueryState 200 per entry) / `Hashes` / `Expected <dir> name|date|type|size` (the expected order computed independently: `StrCmpLogicalW`, folders first), `FMouse` / `FDrag` (mouse messages SENT to the folder window, client coordinates recomputed from its current rect at each step: the move / resize / snapping tests run in a locked session), `Atom` / `UnAtom` (global atoms carry paths and names into DockCommand 136 and ItemCommand 844 / 849 / 850), `MenuWnd` / `MenuItems` (the `#32768` of the dock process and its strings via `MN_GETHMENU` + `GetMenuStringW`); `mkdata.ps1 [-Reset]` builds the test folders under `p12\data` (f80: 80 files with unique sizes / dates + 2 folders + a hidden file, fa, fb, ext; `Paint.lnk` must target `%LocalAppData%\Microsoft\WindowsApps\mspaint.exe`: there is no `System32\mspaint.exe` on this build); scripts `t-smoke.ps1`, `t-12a.ps1`, `t-12a-settings.ps1` (dot-sources `c1\h.ps1` FIRST for the `C1` list-view helpers, then `h12.ps1`, whose functions must win), `t-restart.ps1`, `t-user.ps1`; the spike `p12\spike\spike.exe T|C`). The user's config backup for phase 12 is `p12\config.user.bak.json`. Shell change notifications for files created by a script arrive about 1.3 s later (the shell batches interrupt-level events): wait for the entry count, then check that exactly one reload happened. Never drop or drag into the user's folders; the drop tests use ItemCommand 848 - 850 (the drop code without OLE's drag loop, which needs real input) while the session is locked. Recycle tests: recycle only `p12\data\fa\recycle-me.txt`, then take it out of the bin with `Shell.Application` (`Namespace(10)`, matched by name AND original location) into `p12\restored` and delete that folder. In the locked session `SetForegroundWindow` (to Progman or a test window) fails and `SetWindowPos(HWND_TOP)` from the dock's process was refused too; z-order tests use `SetWindowPos` cross-process on the folder window and `GetWindow` walks (`Above`). `p6\wdtest\wdtest.exe` needs a title without spaces (with spaces the arguments shift and it exits at once). 12b: `t-12b.ps1` starts the dock with **`WINDOCK_DESKTOP_DIR=p12\data\desk`** (the fake Desktop the rules watch and sort; set in the test's own environment just for `Start-Process`, so the user's real Desktop is never watched or touched) and drives the Folder window page by control id (`SetEdit` = `WM_SETTEXT` with a string through `[P12]::SendMessageS` + `EN_KILLFOCUS`, `CheckCtl`, `ComboCtl`); PowerShell 7's `Get-Clipboard` / `Set-Clipboard` have no file list (`-Format` / `-Path` are Windows PowerShell 5.1 only): `p12\clip.ps1` (run with `powershell.exe -STA`) reads the clipboard's files and "Preferred DropEffect" and runs Explorer's own Copy / Cut / Paste verbs through `Shell.Application`. **While the session is locked no process can open the clipboard** (`OpenClipboard` fails with ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED, also for the dock), so the clipboard checks need an unlocked session; back up the user's clipboard text first (`t-12b.ps1` restores it). The `t-12a-settings.ps1` "older build" check needs a pre-phase-12 exe (it used the dd18f42 release build; `build\x64-release` is a phase 12 build now).
- Phase 14 tests (session scratchpad `p14\`: `h14.ps1` on top of `p13\h13.ps1` with `Cfg14 <items> <edge> <date> <weather json>`, `StartDock14 <exe> @{ ENV = value }` (environment just for the dock process: `WINDOCK_WEATHER_HOST=127.0.0.1:9` is the offline path), `WidgetSlots`, `ClickSlot` (sent mouse messages), `ClickPopupAt` (**sets the cursor first: ScriptPopup reads `GetMessagePos`**, a sent click without it lands at the old cursor position), `PopupWnd` / `WxPopupWnd`, `CropAt`, `WData14`, `BackupCache14` / `RestoreCache14` (a test for another place overwrites `weather-cache.json`: put the user's back), `CleanWidgets14`; the c194d4b build in the worktree `p14\wt-base`, `build-base.cmd`). The user's config backup for phase 14 is `p14\config.user.bak.json` (autoHide off, reserveSpace **false**, seconds on, snapshots, tray on on 2026-11-03). A freshly opened script popup is captured black by PrintWindow in the locked session (as 13's popups): make the widget redraw it (its refresh button: an onChange redraw) before capturing. Rebuild before testing an edited built-in widget: its files are RCDATA in the exe.
- Round 5 phase A tests (session scratchpad `a0f951f6-...\scratchpad\`: `h.ps1` self-contained helpers (QS / DockCmd with the dock in the high word, `Ctl page id` under `WinDock.Settings`, `PickCombo`, `SetEdit` (WM_SETTEXT + EN_KILLFOCUS), `ClickBtn`, `ReadCfg`), `t-a1.ps1` per-item weather / clock QueryStates, `t-a2.ps1` the generated page (DockCommand 142), `t-a3.ps1` migration; config `cfg-a.json`; the user's config backup `config.user.bak.json` and legacy cache `weather-cache.user.bak.json`). The weather caches are per place since round 5 (`weather-cache-<8 hex>.json`): a test deletes the ones it made; the user's old `weather-cache.json` is only read, never written. **A settings page that rebuilds** (a field others depend on via `visibleIf`, the item picker) does it in a posted message: a control handle looked up before that message runs is destroyed under the script; wait ~0.5 s after such a change before driving the page again (a picker change sent right after a `click` change was lost to this once). A new Item window gives the thread's focus to its first control (the search box). Choosing a place starts that place's forecast and writes a new `weather-cache-<hash>.json`: list the caches before a run and delete the new ones after (the geocoder search alone writes none).

Reusable helpers live in `tools\smoke\` now (`WinDock.psm1`; `menu-zorder.ps1`). Under `Set-StrictMode`, `(Get-Process ...).Count` with no process throws: wrap in `@()`.

user); ROADMAP.md's "Done" table lists the commits and its "Not verified" list what still needs an
unlocked session, a second display or the user.

| Sub-phase | Status | Notes |

| 1a Config schema `docks[]` / `items[]`, one-time migration of `pinned[]` and dock-level keys | Done, verified | `DockConfig` / `DockItemEntry` / `ItemKind` in Config.h; pins = pin-kind items (`PinIndexes`, `InsertPin`, `SetPins`); clock / weather shown by their items; legacy file migrated on load and saved at once (`Config::migrated`). Not exercised: Pinned page Move up / down / Remove / Add… (cross-process list selection), file drops (`pinFiles`) |
| 1b `DockElement` model: clock / weather / separators / pins / running apps through one path | Done, verified | `DockElement.h` (interface, `ElementHost`, `SlotBuilder`, `LayoutContext` / `ElementSize` in main / cross terms), `DockElement.cpp` (pin, running), `WidgetElements.cpp` (clock, weather). The dock lays out `docks[0].items[]` in config order: each element contributes slots (`DockItem`, kinds 4 clock / 5 weather added), widgets are non-magnifying slots sized by `Measure`, spacers (`DockItem::lead`) 17 DIP icon <-> widget and 10 DIP widget <-> widget, hairlines in the background only for widgets at an end of the dock (`separatorX` right, `leftSeparatorX` left; `Theme::DrawShelf` takes the second one). Input goes through the element (press / click / middle / wheel / right click, popup kind + anchor); the clock tick and weather data are `DockEvent`s that redraw only the element's slots. No `running` item: unpinned apps are not shown. Drag: pins are refilled into the pins' places (a pin can move past a widget), widgets are never dragged or drop places; file drops insert before a pin / after the last pin. 1,063,936-byte exe. Rest-state screenshots differ from d0713d3 by <= 3 levels in ~400-900 icon-edge pixels: surface atlas placement (the same signature appears between two d0713d3 runs with a different allocation history, see cautions). Not verified: a real DPI change, the item menu commands run on widgets other than 800 / 801 / 806, open-with drops on an app, middle click on an app (would start a new instance) |
| 1c Phase 0 quick wins, edit mode (drag any element), lock, Settings "Dock contents" page | Done, verified | Quick wins: `clock.showSeconds` (`Clock::EffectiveFormat` adds `:%S` after the last `%M` / `%R`; a format with seconds is kept), `clock.click` `"calendar"` / `"launch"` / `"none"` + `clock.clickTarget` (`Launcher::OpenTarget`: ShellExecuteEx, `%VAR%` expanded, quoted path + arguments, URLs; without `click` an old `"calendarPopup": false` reads as none and is no longer written), the calendar popup's **Open …** footer button whenever a target is set (300 -> 336 px tall), per dock `previewsSingleWindow`, `middleClick` (`newInstance` / `minimize` / `close` / `none`, apps only; folders and the bin still open in Explorer) and `wheel` (`none` / `cycle`, one window per notch, partial deltas add up) routed through `DockElement::OnMiddleClick` / `OnWheel` (`ElementHost::DockCfg`, `SlotWindows`, `OpenTarget`; `Activation::CycleItem`). Settings: General (Show seconds 2132, Clicking the clock opens 2135, App or URL 2137 + Browse… 2138; 2130 / 2131 retired), Behaviour (single-window previews 2223, Mouse: 2227 / 2229). Edit mode: widgets drag like icons (`UpdateElementDragTarget`: in front of the first other unit whose rest centre is right of the cursor, the running apps one block; `PinRequests::moveItem` -> `DockConfig::MoveItem`); the dragged widget's visual moves into `itemsGroup_` for the drag; pins and running apps keep the 1b refill rules; the drag layout now computes every order's spacers (fixes 1b's running-app-pinned-across-a-widget spacing, compared against 8f63bfd by screenshot). Not verified: a real wheel on a precision touchpad (only 120 / 40 deltas), `clickTarget` URLs and quoted paths with arguments (code path only), middle click / wheel on a pinned (not running-only) app slot (same helper), the widget landing animation when the width does not change, a real DPI change |
| 2a Axis layout, edges, alignment / offset, popups per edge | Done, verified | `DockGeometry.h` is the one mapping layer: the layout is canonical (u along the bar, v across it toward the screen edge; bottom = identity, top mirrors v, left / right swap the axes, the main axis is never flipped); `Layout::Phys` / `PhysRect` / `CrossOrigin` / `Canon` / `StageToScreen` map it, `DrawCanonical` draws the bar, hairlines, separators, dots and the drop marker through `DockGeometry::Matrix`, visuals get main offsets driven (`SetMainOffset`) and cross offsets static (`SetCrossOffset`), icon boxes scale about their edge-facing side (`FootOf`). Config per dock: `edge` (enum `DockEdge`, unknown text = bottom), `align` (`DockAlign` center / start / end, 8 DIP from the monitor end), `offset` (DIPs, + = right / down); the bar is kept on the monitor and the stage cut at its ends (`growRoomStart / End`: the bar grows away from a monitor end). The monitor comes from `DockWindow::MonitorRects` (rcMonitor + rcWork; popups clamp to the work area). Vertical docks: stage cross = magnified icon + room for a 340 DIP label (`kVerticalWindowExtra`), label beside the icon toward the screen centre, popups beside the dock (`Layout::popupY` = magnified icon top), reflections off; clock stacks one word per line (`Clock::SetStacked`), weather glyph above the temperature (Measure returns height as main). Top docks: reflection = the icon drawn flipped, reflected, shown mirrored above the icon. Perspective about X (horizontal) / Y (vertical) through the bar's edge side, the far side receding. Bounce / slide / backdrop / reveal strip / overlap column / hit test / drags / drops / QueryItemPoint all through the mapping; the slide tween (`rootY_`) holds the signed physical offset. `PopupAnchor {edge, along, side}` replaces `anchorX, bottom` in `PopupWindow::ShowAt` and the four popups (the folder popup's rows / columns use the room on its side). | 6a Unstacked windows, titles under icons, minimize-to-dock, live preview titles | Done, verified | Per dock `windows` (`stacked` default / `unstacked`), `titles` (`off` default / `one` / `two`), `titlesVertical` (`beside` default / `off`). **Unstacked**: `DockItem::window` (the one window of a window slot; slots are matched by key + window in `SlotBuilder::Add`, `Rebuild`'s structural test, `RefreshPreview`, the preview suppression), `RunningElement` adds one slot per window of each unpinned app (app order kept, windows in first-seen order), `PinElement` keeps the pin slot as it was (click launches / activates / cycles, the app's dot and preview) and appends one slot per window of the pinned app right after it (rule documented in README "Running apps: windows and titles"). A window slot: `windows` = {its window}, click = `Activation::ActivateItem` on that window (front, or minimize when it is in front), preview = that window (eligible with one window), label = its caption, dot = per window (bright when active, attention per window, none while minimized: minimize-to-dock, the slot stays until the window closes), not draggable (`Draggable`), `IndexOfWindow` / `QueryItemPoint(hwnd)` / `ItemCommand(hwnd)` prefer it. The tracker's `Group` gained parallel `minimized` / `attention` vectors and an `EVENT_SYSTEM_MINIMIZESTART..END` out-of-context hook (the shell hook reports no minimize); launch feedback counts the app's windows (`appWindows`), not the slot's. **Titles**: per-slot `caption` layer in `itemsGroup_` (like the dot: unscaled, its main offset driven by the icon's `tx` tween, cross offset static), created only while titles are on (off: no visual, nothing drawn); `Layout::caption*` band = 2 DIP + 1 / 2 lines of Segoe UI 10 DIP under the icon and its reflection (vertical: a 76 DIP column beside the icon toward the screen edge, right-aligned on a left dock), added to the bar's and the stage's cross size; text = window caption for window slots, else the display name, colour `theme.clockText`, one line `NO_WRAP` + ellipsis trimming, two lines wrapped and cut by hand (`RenderCaption`). Titles do not scale with magnification (the icon grows away from them). **Live titles**: `HSHELL_REDRAW` of a tracked window is recorded and compared at the next coalesced reconcile (`Tracked::title`); real changes go to `WindowTracker::TitleFn` -> `DockManager` -> `DockWindow::OnWindowTitles` (window slots' captions / label, `PreviewPopup::UpdateTitles` repaints changed cards) -> one `DrawFrame(false)`. No timer, no polling. Automation: QueryState 70 - 78, ItemCommand 810 (click a slot by index). Settings: Behaviour > Running apps (2230 - 2237). Verified (scratchpad `p6\t-cmp.ps1`, `p6\t-6a.ps1`, dev build): stacked default vs a 12241cc build on the same config: every `QueryItemPoint` + kind, rest and magnified window rects, hover, QueryState 34 identical, screenshots identical except the clock's minute; unstacked with two mspaint windows + a WinForms window: pin slot first, the two Paint windows right after it, the form its own slot; 810 and a real click activate each window, the dot follows the active window, a second click minimizes (slot kept, dot 0), a third restores; the pin's click activates Paint; the hover label shows the window title (length via QS 74), the preview shows one card (that window); a `WM_SETTEXT` rename updated the label within 250 ms (QS 76 +1, pending timers unchanged), the open preview card (QS 75) on an unstacked and a stacked dock, and the caption under the icon (QS 77; QS 5 = 0); closing a window removed its slot. Titles one / two lines on bottom, top, left, right docks at rest and magnified (screenshots `p6\out\6a-*`), titlesVertical off gives 0 lines on a left dock, reflection height (QS 34) 36 with and without titles; Settings combos 2232 / 2234 / 2236 apply live and save. 2,904,576-byte exe (+21 KB). Not tested: a real DPI change with titles; HSHELL_REDRAW from UWP (CoreWindow) apps' title changes |

Verified (`p6\t-6b.ps1`, `p6\t-6b-settings.ps1`, test windows of an own exe `p6\wdtest\wdtest.exe` pinned so no user window is captured): live on bottom / left docks with two mspaint + an animated + a still test window: 4 sessions, every tile a frame, pins stay icons, 9.5 - 9.6 fps for the animated window (QS 81), CPU 31 - 140 ms per 10 s shown (0.3 - 1.4 %) vs 0 - 16 ms hidden, GPU 3D 0.11 % shown vs 0 - 0.002 % hidden; hidden: 0 sessions, no frames; shown again: 4 sessions; magnified tile, click activates its window, minimized keeps its (dimmed) frame; stacked: the app slot shows its active window, one session per app; snapshots (3 s): exactly 4 rounds in 12 s with 4 PrintWindow each (16), 3 per round with one window minimized (its frame kept), none hidden, QS 5 = 0, CPU 0 - 31 ms per 12 s; 20 open / close cycles per mode: live private bytes 90.5 - 91.1 MB (last 10 within 0.2 MB, sessions back to 4), snapshots 91.0 - 91.7 MB (last 10 within 0.3 MB); off: no tiles / sessions; Settings combo switches live on at run time (device rebuilt, 2 sessions, frames), to snapshots and off, saves. Default config (thumbnails off): `t-cmp.ps1` vs 12241cc pixel-identical at rest and magnified (0 differing pixels), 0 ms CPU over 10 s idle, no timer; `t-6a.ps1` all pass again. 2,938,368-byte exe (+34 KB). Middle click: nothing. Running members: **dot only** (the decision): the group slot shows a dot while a member runs (active / attention from the members' windows, QueryState 78), the member apps still get their own running slots; no previews on the group. Creating: hold-to-group in the pin drag (`GroupCandidateAt`: the dragged app / folder (pinned, or running with an identity) over the middle half of another pinned app / folder or a group, on the icon row; the order resets to rest so the target stays under the pointer; a pin arms after `kGroupHoldMs` = 600 ms (timer 23), a group at once; the target scales 1.2; release calls `PinRequests::group`). `App`: `GroupWith` (a group of the two at the target's place, or added; a pinned source leaves the pins), `AddToGroup`, `MemberOut` (SIZE_MAX = right after the group), `RemoveMember` (the last member removes the group), `Ungroup` (members become pins at its place), `RenameGroup` (`InputDialog` (new): an in-memory dialog template, edit id 100; dock-owned with the hold + CBT focus fix), `SetItemView` for groups. Menu: Show contents / Rename… / Ungroup / Unpin group and its N shortcuts / icon entries / View. | 11a Scrollable docks | Done, verified (real wheel / hover / OLE drop verified 2026-11-03, see the end of this row; the hovered end icon is drawn faded: open follow-up 1) | Per dock `overflow` (`OverflowMode`: `"scroll"` default / `"shrink"` / `"clip"`, written only when not scroll; Settings > General > Position "When the icons do not fit" 2166 / 2167). **Design**: `DockGeometry::Viewport` is the viewport transform on the main axis (content c shows at c - scroll); the layout's rest positions (`slotX`, `x`, targets) are *view* coordinates, so a scroll (`DockWindow::ShiftScroll`) shifts every rest position by the same amount and hit testing, labels, previews, popups, drags, the drop marker and `QueryItemPoint` needed no change; `ComputeLayoutAt` decides `view.on` when lead + run + trail > the room (monitor extent minus 2 x 8 DIP margins; full width: the edge), the bar takes the room, `growMax` 0 (the background never grows; `bgLeft/RightTarget_` fixed), `growRoomStart/End` unlimited (the magnified run is pushed past the ends and clipped there, so the icon under the pointer stays under it and the shape is the same as on a dock that fits), the hairlines are dropped (the spacers scroll), the title centres on the view. Both slot groups get a main-axis clip at the bar's ends (`ApplyReflectionClip`, combined with the reflection clip). Edge fades = per-slot opacity on the icon container / dot / caption (widgets: their visual) that follows each slot's own placement curve exactly: `DockGeometry::FadeSegments` splits the ease-out (or linear) position cubic where the box centre crosses a ramp boundary and re-expands each piece, so the fade is a piecewise-cubic `IDCompositionAnimation` (`DockWindow::DriveEdgeFade`; a non-scrolling dock makes no call). Slots whose whole path stays outside the view get only bookkeeping (`Renderer::Track`) and are put back on their curve (`Renderer::Resume`, the rest of the cubic) when they come near (`placementStale`). Glides: the wheel 0.2 s, the drag on the bar follows the pointer (throttled like magnification), the fling is an ease-out of 0.55 s whose start speed 3d/D matches the release speed (samples of the last 100 ms; reduce motion: none), all scaled by `animation.speed`. Scroll thumb (`scrollThumb_`, created on first use) along the bar's far side, faded in 0.1 s / out 0.3 s, one-shot timer 24 (900 ms). Auto-scroll timer 25 (30 ms) only while an icon drag or an OLE drag is within one slot of a view end (up to 14 slots / s). `ShowClickPopup` scrolls the slot into view first. `"shrink"`: `ComputeLayout` re-runs `ComputeLayoutAt(iconDip)` with a smaller icon size (16 DIP minimum; `layout_.iconDip`, `TilePx` follow it). | 11b Half-circle and circle docks (polar layout) | Done, verified (real hover / reveal / OLE drop / canvas drag verified 2026-11-03, see the end of this row; the ring acrylic was a grey box: fixed in 6cf81fd, rings draw without the blur) | Per dock `shape` (`DockShape`: `"bar"` default / `"arc"` / `"circle"`), `radius` (DIPs to the icons' centre line, 0 = automatic, 60 - 2000), `rotateIcons`, `x` / `y` (a circle's centre in DIPs from the work area's top-left; `DockConfig::kCentred` = the middle), each written only when not the default. **Design**: `DockGeometry::Polar` is the third mapping beside the edges: the run is laid out by the unchanged bar code (canonical u along it, v across) and wrapped onto the ring, theta = theta0 + dir * u / R (`PolarDirection`: bottom arc pi -> 0, top pi -> 2 pi, left pi/2 -> -pi/2, right pi/2 -> 3 pi/2, circle from the top clockwise), r = R + vRing - v; `Layout::Phys / PhysRect (sector bounds) / BoxRect (upright boxes) / Canon (inverse, unwrapped around the span's middle) / PhysSize / DrawEdge` go through it, so hit testing, InZone (the band), drags, drops, labels' anchors, QueryItemPoint, the scroll viewport and the edge fades are the bar's; a ring is laid out horizontally (`vertical` false) whatever its edge. `ComputeLayoutAt`: auto radius = run / span rounded up (a circle: plus a 1.25-slot gap at the top), clamped to [hole 24 DIP / 1.5 icons, what the monitor (an arc) / the work area (a circle) holds]; an arc is placed along its edge by align / offset with its diameter as the bar (EndDockDrag repeats the arithmetic), a circle at x / y kept on the monitor; the canonical bar is the arc [0, span R]; more than that scrolls (`overflow` scroll and shrink; no thumb, no rectangle clip: the fades take slots out). Placement: `PlaceOnRing` puts each slot's upright box (icon container / widget visual, its dot) at the mapped centre and moves it along the arc with sampled Hermite curves of the slot's own u curve (`DockGeometry::PathSegments`, 1 - 8 pieces), scale about the side facing the centre (grows outward), `rotateIcons` turns icon / iconBig about the box centre (`ApplyIconTurn`). Background: one static ring / half-ring surface (`RenderBackground`, fill + barBorder edges, no shelf / seam / halves). Label: `Polar::Outside` at the icon's angle. Popups: `PopupAnchorAt` picks the side the outward direction mostly points to, `PopupAnchor::alongBias` (new) puts the popup's near corner at the slot when it points diagonally; the preview popup keeps the whole anchor (`previewAnchor_` is a PopupAnchor now). Windows: rest window = the ring's box with a window region of the band (`UpdateWindowRegion`; none while expanded / moving), acrylic = the ring's box cut by a region on the blur window, no app bar, no full width / peek / reflections / perspective / titles. Auto-hide: an arc slides into its edge (the root's offset on the edge's own axis), a circle scales about its centre to a 44 DIP hub at 90 % opacity (`rootScale_` in a root transform group; HiddenRect = the hub). Ctrl + drag: a circle anywhere on its monitor (`PinRequests::moveCircle` -> x / y), an arc along its edge; the Settings > Layout canvas drops a circle at x / y. Not supported: SECONDLY / MINUTELY / HOURLY, BYWEEKNO, BYHOUR / BYMINUTE / BYSECOND (ignored), RANGE=THISANDFUTURE. Tests `tests/ics/*.ics` + `.expected` (the RFC 5545 3.8.5.3 examples incl. WKST MO / SU, 1FR, 1SU / -1SU, BYSETPOS -1, election day, Friday 13th; DST NY spring / fall / gap, Berlin -1SU, fallbacks; EXDATE / RDATE / overrides / all-day / floating / DURATION / escapes / folding), runner `tests/ics/IcsTests.cpp`, CMake target `WinDockTests` (EXCLUDE_FROM_ALL): 3 case files, 76 occurrences, 0 failures. **Service** `CalendarService.h/.cpp` (owned by DockManager, `DockShared::Calendar()`): config `calendars { feeds[{name, url, color, enabled, refreshMin}], showDots, defaultReminderMin }` (written only when not default; `DefaultFeedColor` palette); nothing exists without an enabled feed. Web feeds (http / https / webcal -> https): `CreateThread` job per fetch, `Http::Perform` (Result gained `etag` / `lastModified`), 15 s, 32 MB, parsed on the worker, If-None-Match / If-Modified-Since when data exists, cache `calendar-cache\<fnv64>.ics` + `.meta` (url, ETag, Last-Modified, fetch time; loaded at start, due = fetch time + interval), one coalesced one-shot timer (owner timer 40) for the earliest due feed, backoff 1, 2, 4 ... min capped at refreshMin. File feeds: read on a worker; one watcher thread `ReadDirectoryChangesW` (non-recursive, file names matched in the thread) -> `WM_APP+51` -> 300 ms debounce timer 41 -> re-read if the stamp changed. Results `WM_APP+50` (generation + feed id checked). Expansion on the UI thread: [today-31, today+92] local days, `EnsureDays` extends (bounded ~3 years), `byDay_` index (all-day and continuing first); `WM_TIMECHANGE` re-expands. **Popup** `CalendarPopup`: without a running service exactly as before (checked: user's config, HEAD vs new popup rect and PrintWindow pixels identical); with one: up to 3 feed-colour dots per day, day cells clickable (neighbouring month: navigates), selected day ring, the day's header + `ItemGridView` list (colour-bar bitmap icon, title, start time / All day / Until), up to 6 rows within the room, then scrolls; detail strip (range, feed, location, description) for the hovered / Up-Down-selected row; click / Enter opens an http(s) `URL` (OpenTarget), else nothing; `ShowAt` again on every selection so it grows / shrinks. Clock: `ClockElement` adds a 5 DIP accent dot after the last line (under it on vertical docks) while today has events and showDots (`DockEvent::Calendar`, re-checked on each tick). on rings), b069f31 (the work-area fix fought third-party app bars and froze the dock for seconds).
Idle with the user's own config after the run: 188 ms CPU in 30 s, 0 widget wake-ups; the same

### Open follow-ups (found 2026-11-03 verification)
1. **Scrolling dock: the hovered end icon is drawn faded.** Repro: `verify\v11a-fade.ps1` (60 pins,
   96 DIP icons, bottom). At scroll offset 0 hover slot 0: it is drawn at 40 % opacity (QueryState
   141 0 = 40) though nothing lies before it (at rest at offset 0 it is 100 %); hover slot 1 and
   slot 0 drops to 12 %; the same at the far end at offset max (slot 59: 40 %). Cause: any growth
   past an end (`growLeft > 0.5`) turns that end's fade on (`ComputeTargets` `viewEnds`), and the
   1.25-slot ramp is measured from the view's start, where slot 0's centre always sits. Options:
   no fade at an end the run is scrolled to (only clip what is pushed past it), or ramp only the
   pushed-out distance. A design choice (the 11a notes call the push fade intended), so not changed.
2. **Ring docks have no blur any more** (6cf81fd): a DWM system backdrop ignores the blur window's
   window region on this build, so a ring cannot use the `WinDockBackdrop` window. A blurred ring
   would need another way to shape the backdrop (e.g. a blur of our own, or clipping through
   DirectComposition), if wanted.
3. **TaskbarController still gives the taskbar's own edge back by force**: a third-party app bar on
   the *same* edge as the hidden taskbar would still be fought (six SPI_SETWORKAREA broadcasts after
   a hide). Each broadcast is synchronous; one slow window (here a VS Code updater's `.tmp`
   process) makes it take ~2.8 s on the UI thread. Repro: `verify\appbar.ps1` with `$d.uEdge = 3`
   (bottom), start a dock, watch `p8\dbwin.ps1` for "keeps the taskbar's space". Not seen in use.
4. **Open faces folder** (Settings > General, `ShellExecute` "open" of the folder) is expected to
   open a tab in the user's open Explorer window on this build (the `explorer.exe C:\` caution), so
   a test could not close what it opened without touching the user's window: left unverified by
   real input (the user can click it once).

### After round 4: the volume bar (2026-11-03)
A wheel over the Volume or Now playing widget shows a volume bar beside the dock (44 x 150 DIPs:
the level, a vertical track in the accent colour with a knob, the speaker glyph) that follows every
notch and goes 1.2 s after the last one. Host: a second, **passive** script popup kind,
`popup.open(w, h, { passive: true })` (or `{ width, height, passive }`): `ScriptPopup` takes its
mode and class (`WinDockWidgetPassivePopup`), `DockWindow::widgetPassive_` is a hover-mode
`PopupWindow` with the new `SetMouseTransparent` (`WS_EX_TRANSPARENT | WS_EX_LAYERED`, opaque layer
via `SetLayeredWindowAttributes` 255; its acrylic `Backdrop` gets the same styles: WS_EX_TRANSPARENT
alone still took the mouse, `WindowFromPoint` found the bar). No focus, no popup hold, no label
change, not counted by `WidgetPopupShown` (`popup.isOpen`, `closedPopup`) or `ClickPopupOnScreen`;
an open while shown resizes / redraws in place (no fade restart); one per dock; while the widget's
ordinary popup is open a passive open repaints that one instead; an ordinary open, `popup.close()`,
the dock hiding (`StartHide`, desktop layer), the other dock popups' Show*, `ApplyConfig`,
`RecoverDevice`, DPI / display changes and the widget going close it (`DismissWidgetPassive` ->
`onPopupClose({ passive: true })`; ordinary closes now pass `{ passive: false }`). A reload or
failure of the widget also closes it (`Client::WidgetPassiveShown`). `QueryState` 218 = the passive
popup is visible. Widgets: volume `wheelBar` (default on), media `wheelVolume` now default on, on
the 2 % grid and unmuting on the way up like the volume widget. Verified (scratchpad `vb\`: `h.ps1`,
`t.ps1 [-Config config.acrylic.json -Tag acrylic]`, `form.ps1`, `probe2.ps1`; release build, real
cursor and `mouse_event` wheel / clicks, unlocked session; all PASS twice): bar visible after one
notch with WS_EX_NOACTIVATE + WS_EX_TRANSPARENT, 218 = 1 / 65 = 0, the foreground (the user's
Chrome) unchanged, the level +2 % per notch (Core Audio read back), the rect unchanged over notches,
anchored on the widget, `WindowFromPoint` and a real wheel + click over the bar reach an own test
window beneath it, still up at 1.0 s and gone at ~1.4 s; a click right after a wheel opens the sound
popup and the bar is gone; a wheel with the sound popup open moves the level, shows no bar and the
popup survives the bar's timer; the same over the media widget (a middle click opens its popup);
screenshot `vb\out\volume-bar-plain.png`. Testing caution: **the machine was in use during the
first run** (the cursor moved and the volume changed between steps: the first notch landed on the
media widget and a click went wherever the cursor was): `vb\h.ps1` `AtCursor` now refuses to send
a wheel or click unless the real cursor is still exactly where the script put it and the dock is
under it. Left clicks on the Now playing widget send play / pause / next to the user's current
session: tests use its wheel and middle click only.

## Round 5: settings redesign (plan: `C:\Users\olson\.claude\plans\windock-settings-redesign.md`)
Design canvas: "WinDock Settings Redesign". One commit per phase (A fb8b90e, B 465166b, C f790db1, D
the commit after f790db1). **Complete.** Settings now live in three kinds of window that share
nothing but the config: the **Global** window (at most one: General, Themes, Animation, Calendar
feeds, Tray icons, Notifications, Widget library, Docks and displays, About; what applies to every
dock), one **Dock** window per dock (identity header in the dock's colour; tabs Contents (the item
strip + widget drawer + inspector), Position, Behaviour, Running apps, Theme; it edits that dock
only) and one **Item** window per item (a widget, clock, weather, tray, folder or group item: its
own page). Where things moved: the General page's dock controls → the Dock window's Position /
Behaviour / Running apps tabs; Appearance's dock preset → its Theme tab; Dock contents / Pinned apps →
Contents; the Weather page and the General page's clock section → each clock / weather item's own
window (per-item settings, Place / TimeZone fields); Widget settings → each widget's Item window;
Folders and groups → the folder's / group's Item window; Docks + Layout → Global > Docks and
displays; (phase D) the badges and the notification centre check box → the dock's Behaviour tab,
the tray item's grid / icons → the tray item's Item window. No Global page acts on "the selected
dock" any more: the Docks and displays row is only what that page's own buttons act on.

| Phase | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A Per-instance clock and weather (config + runtime + the generated page) | Done, verified (dev and release builds, 2026-11-03) | **Config**: clock / weather items carry `settings` (DockItemEntry::settings, the keys of ClockConfig / WeatherConfig); `ClockOf` / `WeatherOf` / `ScriptWeatherOf` read them over the top-level `clock` / `weather` objects, which stay as the defaults for new items (`Config::EnsureItemSettings` fills a new / old item from them: at load (sets `migrated`, so the file is rewritten), after item changes, on the first run); `ClockSettingsJson` / `WeatherSettingsJson` the writers; ClockConfig + `timeZone`, `label`; WeatherConfig + `label` (each written only when set at the top level); `DockConfig::color` (ARGB, 0 = unset, written only when set) and `Config::DockColor(i)` / `DockPaletteColor(i)` (6-colour palette by index). **Clock**: `Clock::SetTimeZone` (EnumDynamicTimeZoneInformation by key name, `SystemTimeToTzSpecificLocalTimeEx`; "" / unknown = local, the CRT path as before), `SetLabel` (on the date's line, `label · date`, or alone; the time then uses the 14 DIP size), `Clock::TimeZones()` (registry "Display" names, sorted by bias then name), `OffsetMinutes`; the tick is unchanged (minute / second aligned). **Weather**: DockManager holds a registry `weather_` (one WeatherService per latitude / longitude / units / refreshMinutes, `WeatherService::SamePlace`); `SyncWeather` (Sync, ApplyConfig, ReloadPins, ApplyWeather, posted widget requests) creates / configures the wanted places (shown docks' configured weather items + widget users) and stops / drops the rest; per-service timer ids 0x5700 + n on the owner window and process-wide generations, so a result message belongs to one service (an orphan result is freed by the manager); per-place cache `weather-cache-<FNV-1a of "lat,lon,units">.json` (the old single file read as a fallback); `WeatherService::TotalFetches`. DockShared: `FindWeather(place)`, `SyncWeather()`, `RefreshWeather(place / all)` replace `WeatherData` / `ConfigureWeather` / `AnyWeatherItem`. The weather element and popup use their item's place (the popup asks a source lambda for the service of the slot that opened it, `weatherKey_`; `WeatherPopup::Style::title`); the element draws the item's label under the temperature; two clocks have their own calendar footer target (`calendarClock_` / `calendarTarget_`). **Widgets**: `WidgetInstance` carries its weather place (`ScriptWeatherOf`: its own place, else the dock's first weather item, else the defaults; set at creation and on every element rebuild); `WidgetRuntime::UseWeather` resolves the place's service (created on first use), `NotifyWeather(place)` reaches that place's users only; `weather.get()` adds `label`; widgets/weather: `place` / `label` / `units` / `refreshMinutes` fields, draws the label, the popup title. **Schema**: `SettingField::Type::Place` (no key of its own: the item's latitude / longitude / location; ids 3610 - 3626) and TimeZone (Local time + 145 zones here), Reset to defaults (clock / weather: the top-level defaults), Open faces folder (3627) for a clock; the Weather page is gone (`SettingsPagesWeather.cpp` deleted; index 6 is a `RetiredPage` with `InNav()` false so later page ids stay) and the General page's clock section is gone (ids 2112 - 2118, 2127, 2130 - 2139, 2175 - 2178, 2700 - 2727 retired); Dock contents Settings… on a clock / weather row opens the generated page; `App::OpenItemSettings(dock, item)` is the one way in (map pin, `weather.openSettings()`, the popup's button, DockCommand 113 / 142; Phase B changes its body only); SettingsHost `weatherStatus(place)` / `refreshWeather(place)`. **Automation**: QueryState 27 - 30 take the config item index in `lParam` (0: the first weather item as before), 219 a clock item's zone offset (minutes from local), 220 live weather services, 221 total fetches (214 = the same); ## Round 6: desktop layers, floating docks, standalone widgets, notes, performance monitors, drawer previews (plan: `C:\Users\olson\.claude\plans\windock-round6.md`)
One commit per phase (Opus 5.5 agents, Fable review between). ROADMAP.md "Round 6" has the scope table.

| Phase | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 6A Layers, floating docks, dashboard hotkey | Done, verified (dev and release builds, 2026-11-03, unlocked session) | **Layer `"behind"`** (`DockLayer::Behind`; Settings "On the desktop, under every window"; the combo's other rows now read "Normal (above windows)" and "On the desktop, only while the desktop is showing", with a note line 2192 that follows the selection): `DockWindow::SyncLayer` registers the dock with `DesktopLayer` (its Backdrop window as `under`: a behind dock keeps its acrylic; `"desktop"` still has none) after `HWND_NOTOPMOST`, `WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGING` goes through `FilterPosChanging` while registered, `ApplyWindowPos` / `AssertTopmost` use `DesktopLayer::Place`; unregistered when the layer changes, for dashboard mode and on `WM_DESTROY`. `ApplyConfig` re-places a dock whose layer changed (Settings, Reload config); `DockManager::OnExplorerRestarted` = `DesktopLayer::Reassert` (registered behind docks and folder windows) + `ReassertLayer` (shown desktop docks). No app bar on either desktop layer. **Floating docks** (`docks[].floating`, written only when true; `DockConfig::Floating()` = floating && bar && a dock; Sanitize clears it for a ring and forces `autoHide` off; `AutoHideOn()` is false for a floating dock and `App::SetAutoHide` keeps it off): `ComputeLayoutAt` places the bar at `x` / `y` (its resting top-left, DIPs from the work area, `kCentred` = centred, kept on the monitor) and treats it as an edge dock whose screen edge is the bar's outer side (`edgeLine`, margin 0, no full width), so magnification, labels, popups, backdrop, effects and titles are the edge code; `HiddenRect` / `UpdateAppBar` know it.
