| 6C Note widget | Done, verified (dev and release builds, 2026-11-03, unlocked session) | **`TextEditorWindow`** (new `src/TextEditorWindow.h/.cpp`, class `WinDock.TextEditor`): a non-modal, resizable top-level window owned by the widget's host window (the DockWindow or the WidgetWindow), light / dark like the Console (`DarkMode::CurrentPalette`, `SetDarkCaption`, buttons `ThemeControl`, the multi-line EDIT's scroll bar `DarkMode_Explorer`, the single-line EDIT `DarkMode_CFD`, `WM_CTLCOLOR*` from the palette; `ImmersiveColorSet` re-themes), Segoe UI 10 pt edit, the message font for OK / Cancel / the status line; 420 x 320 DIP (single-line 420 x 140, height fixed), minimum 280 x 170, the size remembered per widget id (+ "|line") for the session; placed on the host's popup side of the widget's box (`PlaceNear`: above a bottom dock's slot / a standalone window, below a top dock's, right of a left dock's, left of a right dock's; else the opposite side, else beside; clamped to the box monitor's work area), at that monitor's DPI, `WM_DPICHANGED` re-fonts and lays out; `WS_EX_TOPMOST` only when the owner is topmost (a normal-layer dock or widget, or one lifted by the dashboard); `SetForegroundWindow` + focus in the text. Keys by subclassing (no dialog manager): Escape = Cancel, Ctrl+Enter = OK (single-line: Enter too), Tab / Shift+Tab cycle text / OK / Cancel (no tab characters), Ctrl+A selects all, Ctrl+Backspace deletes a word, Enter on a focused button presses it, the control's own Ctrl+Z (its undo buffer emptied after the initial text); the characters of those keys never reach the text. Status line "n characters" (a line break counts one: `EM_SETLIMITTEXT` = maxLength + the line breaks, updated on `EN_CHANGE`), "n characters (the limit)" in an amber colour at the maximum. Placeholder: `EM_SETCUEBANNER` (single-line) or painted after `WM_PAINT` in the disabled-text colour (multi-line). Self-deleting at `WM_NCDESTROY`; `done(ok, text)` runs once (OK / Cancel / the close box / the window destroyed with its owner) unless `Abandon()`ed; no timers. **Script API**: `popup.editText(options)` (native `EditTextFn` in `kPopupFuncs`): a Promise of the text (`\r\n` -> `\n`) or `null`; bad arguments (not an object, a non-string title / text / placeholder, maxLength not a number in 1 - 1000000, text longer than maxLength) reject with `TypeError` / `RangeError`, "an editor is already open" / "there is no editor for this widget here" with `Error` (a rejected promise, never a throw: `QuickJsEngine::Rejected`). `ScriptHost::EditText(request, error)` -> id; `WidgetInstance` keeps the request (`editPending_`), the host's posted popup handler (`DockWindow::OnWidgetPopupRequests`, `WidgetWindow::OnPopupRequest`) calls `OpenPendingEditor()` (never inside a script call), which asks `Client::WidgetEditorPlace` (owner, box in screen px, topmost, side) and opens the editor; the editor's `done` -> `OnEditorDone` -> `ScriptEngine::Settle(id, true, text JSON | "null")` (new: a second pending map `settles_`, resolved through the pumped job queue like http), generation-checked; `Reload` / `Fail` / the destructor drop the editor (`DropEditor`: no settle, the old script is gone). One editor per instance. `dock.itemKey` (new `ScriptDockInfo::itemKey`, DockGet 17): `"<dock id>/<widget id>#<n>"` (the instance key, so it survives moves past other items) on a dock, the entry id standalone (decision: the plan's "<dockId>/<item index>" would change on every move past any item). `storage.onChange(fn)` (new, in the prelude): `WidgetRuntime::StorageSet / StorageRemove` with the writer post `kStorageMessage` (WM_APP + 36, coalesced per key, only when another instance of the widget id exists and the value changed) and `Emit("storage", { key })` to the other instances. **Dock / window**: `Client::WidgetEditorChanged` -> DockWindow hold bit `kHoldEditor` (1 << 6; revealed with `StartShow` when hidden), `editorWidget_`; `ApplyWindowPos` / `AssertTopmost` leave the z-order alone while an editor is open (8.2's lesson: `HWND_TOPMOST` + `SWP_NOOWNERZORDER` would put the dock above its own window); `BuildElements` releases the hold when the widget went; WidgetWindow's `AssertZOrder` skips `HWND_TOPMOST` while its editor is open. **Notes widget** (`widgets/notes`, built in: RCDATA 344 / 345): storage `note:<itemKey>` or `note:id:<noteId>` = `{ text, modified }`; dock tile: title (bold) + up to `lines` lines that fit the icon-size height (font shrinks to 7 DIPs at least), `measure()` width from the widest line up to `maxWidth`, wheel scrolls the lines (a mark shows where), a left / right dock a square title tile (the font shrinks so the longest word fits); standalone: the whole note wrapped with 10 DIPs padding, wheel scroll with a bar; "New note" placeholder; paper none / yellow (default) / pink / blue / theme (label panel) as a rounded sheet with a shadow line; `textColor` `#00000000` = automatic (a colour field cannot be empty); `noteId` shares a note (redrawn through `storage.onChange`); `dock.preview` shows a sample (6E). Click -> `popup.editText({ title: 'Note', maxLength: 20000 })`. Standalone 220 x 180, min 80 x 60. `examples/edit-text` (new). `tools/check_widget_docs.py` also reads members the prelude adds to native namespaces (`g.storage.onChange`). **Automation**: QueryState 255 (slot `LOWORD(lParam)`'s editor open 1 / 0, -1 not a script widget; ### Round 7 final regression (2026-11-04)
On the final release exe (4,704,768 bytes, commit 0da1cd3), each script in its own `pwsh`: `p7a\t-7a.ps1` **11 PASS, 0 FAIL** (menu, popup gap 6 / 20 px, maxLength 400 scrolling / 0 hugging, Settings round trips, the real-pointer label + preview check), `p7b\t-7b.ps1` **13 PASS, 0 FAIL** (unanchored dock unchanged, the anchored left dock's groups and offsets, ItemCommand 859 / 860, the inspector's combo and offset, overflow wins over anchors, the horizontal end group), `p7c\t-7c.ps1` ran on this exe in 7C (11 PASS). The user's config was restored byte for byte (hash `AF510DB3…`) and the user's dock restarted from the release exe. Round 7 is complete; the phases' "Not verified" lists are the open follow-ups.

## Round 8: settings decluttering (plan: `C:\Users\olson\.claude\plans\windock-round8.md`)
One commit per phase (Opus 5.5 agents, Fable review between). ROADMAP.md "Round 8" has the scope table. The user's constraint: the Contents tab's widget drawer keeps its live previews. Testing is light (one or two checks per change; the regression is phase 8E).

| 8A Foundations | Done, verified (dev and release builds, 2026-11-04, locked session) | **Reflow** (`SettingsWindow::Reflow`, run by every `LayoutPage`): `Ctl` gained `base` (as built / `MoveCtl`), `row` (the builders' row index: `NextRow` increments it, `Section` / `Title` / `EndCard` / `EndExpander` start a row, `Radios` one row per radio), `card`, `expander`, `measured`, `maxLines`, `collapsed`, `shown`, `tip`. Each pass derives `dip` from `base`: horizontally, a control ending at the build width's right margin stretches with the page (a narrow one in the right half moves, never over its row's other controls; one ending just before a moving one stretches too; text controls are clamped to the margin); vertically, rows keep their build order and gaps and shift by what the rows above grew or shrank: a `Text(Note / Value)` that is not single-line is measured (`DrawText` `DT_CALCRECT | DT_WORDBREAK | DT_EDITCONTROL` in the window's font at the DPI; `SS_EDITCONTROL` so long words such as paths wrap), `maxLines` caps it (the shown text is cut at a word with "…", `Ctl::text` keeps the full one), a row whose controls are all gone (`Collapse(id or ids, hidden)` (a check box with its caption, an edit with its up-down, a label with its hint), or inside a closed `Expander`) takes no height (its pitch, not the gap before a following card). `SetText` of a measured note relayouts when its height changes. ## Round 9: full-screen apps, steady magnification, clearer auto-hide, settings search, presets, help (plan: `C:\Users\olson\.claude\plans\windock-round9.md`)
One commit per phase (Opus 5.5 agents, Fable review between). ROADMAP.md "Round 9" has the scope table. From the user's pasted wish list of 2026-11-04; testing is light (one or two checks per change; the regression is phase 9E).

| Phase | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 9A Dock fixes | Done, verified (dev and release builds, 2026-11-04, unlocked session) | **Full-screen guard** (`DockManager`, `settings.fullscreen` "hide" default / "ignore"; `SettingsUiConfig::fullscreenHide`, written only as `"fullscreen": "ignore"`): the foreground watch runs whenever the guard is on (`WatchWanted()`: intelligent docks, the dashboard, "desktop" widgets, the guard; the hooks fire only on events, nothing polls) and `EvaluateFullscreen()` runs first in every watch callback, at the end of every `Sync()` (`SyncFullscreenGuard`), after the dashboard ends and on an app bar's `ABN_FULLSCREENAPP` (`DockShared::FullscreenNotice`; with "ignore" the dock keeps its old answer: HWND_NOTOPMOST + HWND_BOTTOM). **Rule** (`FullscreenMonitors`): the foreground window's root, if not ours, not `WindowTracker::IsExcludedClass`, visible, not cloaked, not iconic: (1) every monitor its DWM extended frame bounds (else `GetWindowRect`) cover (`<=` on every side) unless it is `IsZoomed` with `(GWL_STYLE & WS_CAPTION) == WS_CAPTION`; (2) else its monitor (`MonitorFromWindow` nearest) when `SHQueryUserNotificationState` is `QUNS_RUNNING_D3D_FULL_SCREEN` or `QUNS_PRESENTATION_MODE`; (3) its monitor when an app bar dock there got `ABN_FULLSCREENAPP` on while this same window was in front (stored as monitor + window, so a notice whose "closed" never arrives cannot hide the docks for another window). DockCommand 150 forces 1 (every monitor) / 0 (none) until 2. Every instance whose monitor is full screen and that is in the topmost band (`TopmostBand()`: layer normal, or lifted by the dashboard; so docks of every auto-hide mode, the tray dock, floating docks and standalone widgets on "normal"; not "desktop" / "behind", not folder windows) gets `SetFullscreenHidden(true)`: DockWindow closes the preview, click popups, passive widget popup and the reminder banner (reminders queue and show when it is back; a widget's popup / passive popup request is refused while hidden), ends drags, cancels the hide and reveal timers, `ShowWindow(SW_HIDE)`, `LeaveZone` (magnify timer, hover, label; `ScheduleHide` / `OnOverlapCheck` are no-ops while hidden), hides the acrylic backdrop, tells its widgets they are not visible; `AssertTopmost` returns and `ApplyWindowPos` adds `SWP_NOZORDER` while hidden, `UpdateDesktopShown` does not show it. Back: `SW_SHOWNA` (unless a "desktop" dock's own rule hides it), `AssertTopmost` once, the backdrop restacked, an intelligent dock re-tests its overlap (`OnOverlapCheck(true)`), an "always" dock not under the pointer schedules its hide. WidgetWindow: `fullscreen_` joins the "desktop" rule in `WantHidden()`, `UpdateShown` hides (popups closed) / shows + `AssertZOrder`. General page card "Full-screen apps" 2275, check box 2276 / 2277 "Hide the docks while a full-screen app is in front", hint 2278 "Games keep their full-screen mode" + the whole rule as the tooltip (`SettingsScope::Fullscreen` -> `SyncFullscreenGuard`). QueryState **280** (App: dock or standalone widget `HIWORD` hidden by the guard; -2 no such dock), DockCommand **150** (handled in `App::OnSharedMessage`; 0 while "ignore"). **Steady bar growth** (`docks[].magnifyBar` "steady" default / "follow", `MagnifyBar`, written only when "follow"): `ComputeLayout` sets `Layout::steady` (not for rings, `view.on`, full width, anchored, or magnification 1.0) and `steadyStart` / `steadyEnd` = half each of the magnification's growth (`magGrow`: `growMax` before the drop gap's minimum), the part an end's room (`growRoomStart / End`) cannot take going to the other end; `ComputeTargets` (not the drag / drop-gap layouts) keeps the follow anchor (`growLeft`, the hovered icon under the pointer) clamped to `[delta - steadyEnd, steadyStart]` so the run stays inside the fixed bar (if the run's growth exceeds both ends, the bar grows past them), the background halves' targets use the bar's ends (`barStart` / `barEnd`: = the run's in follow mode) and every non-magnifying item before the first / after the last magnifying slot (widgets and now also a separator next to them) moves with the bar's end, so the slack sits between the icons and them. Appearance tab Size card row "Bar while magnified" 2279 / combo 2280 ("Grows once and stays" / "Follows the pointer") + tooltip. QueryState **281** = `MAKELONG(bgLeftTarget_, bgRightTarget_ + bgSurfaceW - bgLeftTarget_)` px, stage coordinates. **Auto-hide never greyed**: Behaviour's Auto-hide card collapses the radios, their note and the delays for a floating bar and shows the line 2281 ("A floating bar has no auto-hide: …") and the SysLink 2282 "Position tab" (`Window().ShowPage(kTabPosition)`); the delays expander 2269 and its rows are collapsed while auto-hide is off; the preview single / delay rows while previews are off and Titles on vertical docks while titles are off are collapsed (were greyed); choosing a radio re-checks all three from the config. **The stale-state bug**: `SettingsPage::ChangedFor` only applied the change and `App::OnSettingsChanged` refreshes the *other* windows (`RefreshSettings(source)`), so the source window's other tabs kept their state: a Kind change on the Position tab (floating turns auto-hide off) left the Behaviour tab's radios greyed / ungreyed and checked as before until the window was reopened. Fix: `SettingsWindow::PeersChanged(source)` after every change runs the new `SettingsPage::OnPeerChanged()` of the window's other pages under the loading guard; `DockFieldsPage` re-syncs the auto-hide radios, the hidden Floating / Shape controls and `EnableDependents` (no full `Load`: a half-typed value elsewhere stays, lists are not rebuilt). `kFloating` (2189) goes through the same `Changed`; Reload config already reloaded every page (`Refresh`). **Tooltips over greyed controls**: a disabled control gets no mouse input and a tooltip shows nothing for a disabled window, so each page's tooltip window has one more rect tool (`SettingsPage::kDisabledTipId` 0x9A01) that `PageProc`'s `WM_MOUSEMOVE` moves over the disabled control under the pointer with its text (`TTM_NEWTOOLRECTW` / `TTM_UPDATETIPTEXTW`, then `TTM_RELAYEVENT`; elsewhere its rect is empty): a page's `TTM_GETTOOLCOUNT` is one higher than in round 8. **Greyed-control audit** (every `Enable(` / `EnableWindow(` in `SettingsPages*.cpp`, 31 calls in 11 places): Behaviour auto-hide radios (`EnableWindow` while floating), hide / reveal delays, preview delay / single-window previews, Titles on vertical docks: now collapsed, never greyed; tooltips saying why added: Contents inspector Move left / Move right / Remove (2603 / 2604 / 2602: "Greyed for the first / last item", "Select an item in the strip first") and Position on the dock / Offset (2626 / 2628), Widget library Add to dock / Add to the desktop (3506 / 3509: select a widget first), Tray item Move up / down (4123 / 4124) and Reset to Explorer (4113: "Greyed while the takeover is off"), Folders and groups Reset icon (4007: "Greyed while it has no icon of your choosing"), a group's Remove / Move up / Move down (4015 - 4017), Calendar feeds Remove / Save changes (4314 / 4313: no feed selected), Docks and displays Remove (2803: "Greyed for the last dock"); left as they are, with the reason on the page already: Folders and groups' View / Choose icon / Choose image (greyed only when the item is no longer a folder / group: the note says "This item is not a folder or a group any more."), the folder window page's controls (greyed only for an entry that is not a folder window: its info line says "This is not a folder window."), the standalone widget's Window tab (greyed only for an entry that is not a widget, which that window never shows), the tray item page's grid controls (greyed only when the item is no longer a tray item; ### Round 9 final regression (2026-11-04)
On the final release exe (5,116,416 bytes, rebuilt from clean; `build\dev` the same size; zero warnings in both), every round 9 suite again, each script in its own `pwsh` on its phase's test config, through one orchestrator (session scratchpad `f70d5079-…\scratchpad\p9e\`: `run-all.ps1 [-Only names]` backs up the whole `%LocalAppData%\WinDock` folder once, quits the user's dock, and per run copies the run's config in, starts the release dock, runs the script, quits, deletes what the test made and puts back every changed file byte for byte (hash-checked), and checks "no WinDock, Shell_TrayWnd visible"; it restarts the user's dock at the end; logs `p9e\out\<run>.txt`, `out\summary.txt`). Results (PASS / FAIL): p9a `t-guard` 38 / 0, `t-steady -Mode steady` 4 / 0, `t-settings -Part combo` + `t-steady -Mode follow` 5 / 0, `t-settings -Part behaviour` 20 / 0, `t-tip` (TIPID 2603) 1 / 0; p9a2 `t-steady` 16 / **11**, `t-modes` 10 / 0, `t-drag` 6 / 0, `t-popup` 6 / 0, `t-drift` 4 / 0, `t-settings` 5 / 0; p9a3 `t-zoom` 22 / 0, `t-analog` 7 / 0; p9b `t-9b` 54 / 0; p9c `t-9c` 46 / 0, `t-welcome` (a first run: no config file) 6 / 0; p9d `t-9d` 37 / 0, `t-cards` 0 cards without a "?" on all 4 windows (Global, `main` with its Item windows, the standalone widget, the folder window), `t-gaps` 285 = 0 on the same 4. **The 11 p9a2 `t-steady` fails are a stale expectation, not a regression**: the script (written for e7c2320) measures "the grown bar" right after entering over the start widget at the bar's left end; since 5f9dea6 the bar grows only when something zooms, so that value is the rest bar and every later comparison with it fails (the logged values show the bar growing at the first pin and staying constant). Round 10 phase 10D: where a dock or item value falls back to a shared one (read from `Config.h/.cpp`, `Theme.h`, the pages):

| Setting | Config (local / inherited) | Falls back to | The control now |
|---|---|---|---|
| A dock's theme preset | `docks[].themePreset` set / `""` | the app's `theme.preset` (App settings > Themes); the app's inline field overrides apply on top either way | Dock > Appearance > This dock's theme: entry 0 **Use app default (Dark)** (the app theme's name), a preset makes it local; ↺ 5400 "Reset to app default" while local |
| Theme field values | `theme.*` keys set (override bits) / absent | the theme preset's value (app-wide only: a dock has no per-field values) | App settings > Themes rows: ↺ "Reset to the preset's value" as before (no "app default" row exists) |
| A calendar feed's reminder | `calendars.feeds[].reminderMin` 1 - 1440 / 0 (not written) | `calendars.defaultReminderMin` (Display > Default reminder) | the feed form's **Use the default reminder (15 min)** check box (5403); cleared, the minutes row with ↺ 5405 |
| A script widget's weather place | its `settings` `latitude` / `longitude` (+ `location`) / absent | its dock's first Weather item's place, else the top-level `weather` (ScriptWeatherOf) | the Place hint "Its own place" / "This dock's Weather item's" / "The default place" (8C); ↺ 5406 and the footer's Reset to app default 117 while it has its own |
| Clock / weather item settings | every key in the item's `settings` (EnsureItemSettings copies the top-level `clock` / `weather` when the item is made) | (a key missing from a hand-edited file reads the top-level default) | local only: no Use app default (the copy is made once; the top-level objects have no UI); Reset to factory defaults… restores the copy's values |
| Animation, notifications, tray, dashboard, settings.fullscreen | app-wide only | — | no per-dock value: unchanged |
| A dock's badges, delays, sizes, previews, mouse, running apps, layer, edge, … | per dock only (defaults are the struct's) | — | local only: no Use app default; the expanders count values that differ from a new dock's |
| A folder / group's contents layout (`view`) | per item only (Auto default) | — (Folders and groups is the item's own page, not app defaults) | unchanged |
| A dock's thumbnail tile length | `thumbnailSize` 0 = twice the dock's own icon size | the same dock's value, not an app default | unchanged ("0: twice the icon") |
| A dock's identity colour | `color` 0 = the palette by index | a palette, not a setting | unchanged |

## Round 11: the Windows 7 look (plan: `C:\Users\olson\.claude\plans\windock-round11.md`)
One commit per phase. ROADMAP.md "Round 11" has the design notes. From the user's request of 2026-11-06 ("need to be able to customize the dock to the point where it looks almost identical to windows 7"); testing is light (the regression is phase 11D).

| Phase | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 11A Theme: slot buttons, sheen, spacing | Done, verified (dev build, 2026-11-06) | **Eleven theme fields** appended to `Theme::Field` before `Count` (48 of 64 override bits; `WD_FIELD` rows, `ThemeConfig`, clamps in `Config::Sanitize`): `slotStyle` (enum `"none"` / `"buttons"`), `slotRunning` `#30FFFFFF`, `slotActive` `#55FFFFFF`, `slotAttention` `#D0F0A030`, `slotHover` `#28FFFFFF`, `slotBorder` `#66FFFFFF`, `slotCornerRadius` 3 (0 - 16), `slotHotTrack` true, `sheen` 0 (0 - 1, shown as %), `iconGap` 8 (0 - 48), `barPadding` 8 (0 - 24). **Layout** (`DockWindow::ComputeLayoutAt`): `kGap`, `kIconTop`, `kDotFromBottom` and `kBarExtraHeight` are gone; `l.gap` = iconGap, the bar = icon + 2 x barPadding, `Layout::iconTopPx` = 5/8 of barPadding with the dots (5 at 8, as before) or all of it with buttons (centred), the dots' centre 5/8 of it from the edge (5 at 8); the caption slot and a circle's end gap use `l.gap` (they used `kGap`). **Slot buttons**: a new unscaled `DockItem::button` layer per icon slot, created first in `CreateItemVisuals` (behind `layer`), placed like the dot (`setMain` drives it with the same tween, a static translation `buttonShift` = slot inset - box inset, the cross offset at the bar's top + max(1, barPadding / 2)), destroyed / reset with the slot's other layers; `ButtonStateOf` (the QueryState 293 code), `RenderButtons` at the end of every `ApplyTargets` (draws only slots whose state / stacked count / hot point / size changed; no surface for state 0), `RefreshButtons` after `WM_LBUTTONDOWN` / `UP` (pressed has no placement pass); `RenderDot` assigns no content while buttons are on; no buttons on a ring (`l.buttons` false: its dots stay). States: running (windows or a group's member; a minimized-only window slot counts), active, attention (pulse: an `AddRepeat` opacity animation 1 -> 0.55 -> 1 every 0.8 s on the layer, none with reduced motion; the scrolling edge fade skips a pulsing button), +hover (`hovered_`), +pressed (`pressed_` with no drag), +stacked (an app slot with 2+ windows, 1 or 2 outlines 3 DIPs apart, each clipped to the part beyond the one in front). Hot tracking: `cursorV_` (new) with `cursorX_` gives the point; the hovered frame is redrawn only when the whole-pixel point changes, i.e. on mouse moves through `ApplyMagnify` (its 16 ms throttle), never at rest; ItemCommand 834 puts the point on the icon row's centre line. Drawing is shared with the preview: `Theme::DrawSlotButton` (gradient fill top -> 60 % alpha, hover fill, radial light in the icon's dominant colour (`IconCache::DominantColor`; the accent for group tiles / placeholders) 55 % -> 0 at 0.9 x the frame, black 25 % pressed, inner highlight at 40 %, hairline; a hover-only frame at half strength) and `Theme::DrawSheen` (gradient sheen x 35 % -> 0 at 55 %, a hairline at sheen x 60 % one stroke inside the top edge between the corners; in `RenderBackground`'s panel path after the fill, before the border; not on the shelf or a ring). **Preset `aero` ("Aero glass")** in `Theme.cpp` with the plan's values (tint `#B8587BA8`, fallback `#F0496A94`, corners 0, border `#50FFFFFF`, separator `#40FFFFFF`, sheen 0.6, buttons, iconGap 28, barPadding 4, labels `#F2FFFFFF` / `#FF000000` / `#FF767676` radius 3, clock text white, preview hover `#2E5B8DD0` / `#80FFFFFF`, no effects; colours not tuned further). **Themes page**: Sheen, Gap between icons, Bar padding in the Bar group; a new group **Slot buttons** (heading expander `kSectionFieldsSlots` **2425**: 2422 is `kDockThemeNote`; "?" = `guide-settings.md#slot-buttons`; note `kSlotsNote` **5500**) with Slot style and the seven slot* rows, which `Collapse` while the style is None (`UpdateSlotRows` from `UpdateCounts`); tooltips on the eleven new rows (`FieldTip`). The field rows now reach 3476 (SettingsIds comment: 50 rows fit 3000 - 3499; 3400 - 3499 was unused). **Preview strip** (`SettingsPreview.cpp`): gap / padding from the theme (the old numbers at 8 / 8), sheen on the bar only (`fillPanel(shape, sheen)`), buttons instead of dots (Browser is the stacked sample; the hovered sample gets the hover fill and the light at the focus). **QueryState 293** (slot LOWORD's drawn state: 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 + 16 hovered + 32 pressed + 64 stacked; -1 no slot) / **294** (the dock's `iconGap`, DIPs). README (Settings > Themes groups, Themes presets list with `aero`, eleven key rows, "Slot buttons" under Icon effects, Automation 293 / 294 and the 2425 / 5500 / row ids), guide-settings (Aero glass, the Bar rows, a "Slot buttons" section). **Verified** (session scratchpad `24be0065-…\9ab71064-…\scratchpad\r11\`: `h.ps1`, `run.ps1 [-SkipRelease]`, `t-11a.ps1`, `cfg-11a.json` (dock 0 `aero`: full width bottom, 32 DIP icons, magnification 1, previews off, charmap + Paint pins + running apps; dock 1 `plain`: dark, top, charmap / separator / msinfo32), `crop.ps1`; two Paint windows started and closed by the script; logs `out\run1.txt` .. `out\run4.txt`, pictures `out\` (run 4) and `out\run1` - `run3`): run 4: 29 PASS, 0 FAIL: 294 = 28 (aero) / 8 (plain), QueryState 20 = 0 (aero resolves); The user's config was restored byte for byte after each of the four runs (SHA-256 EEA0648D…3BCC62), no new files in the WinDock folder, the Paint windows closed, their dock restarted from `build\x64-release` (round 10's exe) with the tray takeover (QueryState 97 = 1) and `Shell_TrayWnd` hidden as their config wants. **Not verified**: the attention pulse's animation (PrintWindow returned the same frame 400 ms apart: it captures the layer's static opacity, not DWM's animated value; the state is 3 and the animation is attached by code); real pointer moves and hot tracking at the 16 ms rate (the session was locked: only ItemCommand 834 positions ran); the "active" state (no test window owned the foreground in the run: the foreground stayed the user's; the code is the dot's `item.active`); the aero dock's preview strip (Dock window > Appearance) and the preview with `aero` (the Themes page's strip was pictured with dark + buttons); buttons on vertical docks, top docks, a scrolling run (edge fade), with magnification above 1.0, with titles, thumbnails or a shortcut group; `barPadding` / `iconGap` other than 4 / 8 / 28 on the dock; light mode / high contrast / another DPI; the Help window rendering the new guide section (check_docs only); acrylic off (transparency effects off) for `aero`. |
| 11B Widgets: the Start orb, Show desktop, the two-line clock | Done, verified (dev build, 2026-11-06) | **Start 1.1.0** (`widgets/start`): `icon` choice **`orb`** ("Windows 7 orb"), drawn with canvas calls only: the sphere (a radial gradient `#FF2C74C8` -> `#FF1E5FB4` -> `#FF123F80` -> rim `#FF0B2A5C`, a light from below `#B868D0FF` -> 0), the flag (four `canvas.path` panes red `#FFE8402A` / green `#FF52B043` / blue `#FF2C7FD1` / yellow `#FFF6C01B`, each a top-lighter gradient, every horizontal edge the S-curve `amp * cos(pi u)`: the left column lower), an elliptical highlight (white 55 % -> 0 over the upper part), a radial gloss (white 25 %) and a light rim `#80FFFFFF`; the orb is 0.92 x the slot's shorter side at 100 %. Hovered (`onHover`) every sphere colour 20 % towards white, a radial-gradient glow `#60A8D8FF` -> 0 out to min(1.12 R, the box) and the rim `#B0FFFFFF`; pressed (`onPress`: the API has it, so no click flash) 20 % towards black, released by `onClick`, the pointer leaving, or a one-shot 2 s `setTimeout` (a press that turns into a drag gets neither). `size` is now 0 - 100 with **0 = automatic** (the new default: 62 % for the logos / images, the old default, and 100 % for the orb; label "Icon size (0 = automatic)", the hint says the orb looks best at 95 - 100 %); a stored size keeps its meaning. The other icons' hover is unchanged; nothing runs at rest. **Show desktop 1.0.0** (new built-in `widgets/show-desktop`, RCDATA `IDR_WIDGET_SHOWDESKTOP_JSON` / `_MAIN` **355 / 356**, `res/app.rc`, `kBuiltIns`): `click` `desktop` (`win+d`) / `minimize` (`win+m`) on Content; `style` `strip` / `icon` and `width` (6 - 24 DIPs, default 10, shown for the strip only) on Appearance; the strip is `dock.theme.text` at 10 % / 25 % hovered / 40 % pressed with a 1 px hairline at 35 % on the side towards the other items (left; top on a vertical dock); the glyph is a monitor in the text colour (standalone: always the glyph; `preview.settings` `{ "style": "icon" }` so the drawer shows something). ## Round 13: menus you can read, running apps as a widget (plan: `C:\Users\olson\.claude\plans\windock-round13.md`)
One commit per phase, not pushed. ROADMAP.md "Round 13" has the notes. Light testing per phase: a warning-free `build\dev`, the doc checks, and a manual check list for the user; `build\x64-release` is rebuilt only in 13E. Reusable test scripts are in the repo now: `tools\smoke\WinDock.psm1` and one `tools\smoke\<feature>.ps1` per feature.

| Phase | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|

QueryState **324**. New `tools\smoke\WinDock.psm1` (windows by class, the four messages, config backup / restore, start / quit with the taskbar check, menu helpers) and `tools\smoke\menu-zorder.ps1`; CLAUDE.md points at them. |

**Manual check (13A)**, on your own dock:

1. Right-click the dock's background, point at **Add**: the submenu and the menu stay above the dock while the pointer crosses the icons.
2. The same with **Position on screen** (and Auto-hide): above the dock.
3. Right-click an icon, open its **Dock** submenu and move across the dock: the menus stay on top.
4. With the tray on the dock: right-click a tray icon (the dock's Hide / Pin menu) and cross the dock: on top.
5. With auto-hide on: open the dock menu, let the pointer leave and come back: the menu stays above the dock.

**Manual check (13B)**, on your own dock (light mode, then dark mode; then at your other DPI):

1. Right-click the dock's background: icons beside Customize, Dock settings, Add, Position, Auto-hide, Lock, Rename … Quit, WinDock's own icon on **App**; open Add and Tools.
2. Right-click a pinned app (and a running unpinned one): the greyed header shows the app's name with its icon.
3. Right-click a folder (and the Recycle Bin, a group): the header shows its icon (a group without a custom icon: a glyph).
4. Right-click the clock: **Widget: Clock** with a clock glyph, Show calendar, Widget settings…, Remove this widget, Dock.
5. Right-click a script widget (System monitor): **Widget: System monitor**, Widget settings…, Reload widget, Remove this widget.

**Manual check (13C)**, on your own dock (after the 13E release, or a `build\dev` run):

1. Right-click the clock, click **Show seconds**: the clock shows seconds at once; the menu shows it checked next time (**Show date** the same).
2. Right-click the Weather (script) widget: a **Units** submenu with Metric / Imperial, the current one marked; choosing the other changes the widget at once.
3. On a user widget, add `"quick": true` to a bool field in its widget.json, Reload widget: the entry appears with a check mark that toggles the setting.
4. After one of these, the dock menu's first entry reads **Undo "Changed Show seconds of Clock"** (or the field / widget you changed), and Undo puts it back.
5. With Windows in dark mode, start the dock: the dock menu, an item menu (and its Dock submenu) and the tray-icon menu are dark, with light icons; in light mode they are as before.

**Manual check (13D)**, on your own dock (after the 13E release, or a `build\dev` run):

1. Dock settings > Contents: select the running apps, click **Running apps settings…**: its own window opens (Running apps, Spacing, Order, Notifications); right-click a running app that is not pinned: **Running apps settings…** is the first entry and opens the same window.
2. Clear **Use the theme's gap**, set **Gap** 0 and **Padding** 24: the running apps close up and move 24 DIPs away from the pins and the next widget, at once; magnify across them.
3. **Order**: By name: the running apps sort alphabetically; As they appear puts them back in start order.
4. Dock settings > Behaviour: no badges check; the line "Window slots, titles, thumbnails and badges are set on the Running apps widget (Add > Running apps)." is under the notification centre note; the panel has four tabs.
5. After the first start on this build, `%LocalAppData%\WinDock\config.json`: your dock has no `windows` / `titles` / `runningFilter` / `thumbnails…` / `badges` keys; they are inside its `{ "kind": "running", "settings": { … } }` item.

### Round 13 final regression (2026-11-09)

Phase 13E, on 2d91768 (13D2). Light by design (the plan's "How this round is run"): static checks, the three smoke scripts once each, the release build. No subagents, no screenshots, no real input.

**Static checks**: `build\dev` (WinDock + WinDockTests) up to date and warning-free; `python tools\check_docs.py` clean (79 help topics); `python tools\check_widget_docs.py` no drift; `build\dev\WinDockTests.exe` 3 case files, 0 failures (and the release build's: the same); `build\dev\WinDock.exe --validate-widget` exit 0 "OK: no problems" on all 16 `widgets\*` (agenda, analog-clock, analog-date, battery, digital-clock, hello, media, notes, notification-bell, search, show-desktop, start, system-monitor, system-overview, volume, weather) and all 4 `examples\*` (edit-text, http-json, minimal, popup-list).

**Smoke scripts** on `build\dev\WinDock.exe` (each backs up and restores the config byte for byte; run with `-NoRestart` one after the other, the user's dock quit before the first and restarted by the release step): `tools\smoke\menu-zorder.ps1` 12 / 12 PASS (324 0 → 1 → 0; both menu windows above the dock at z 10 / 11 vs 14; `#32768` under the submenu's first entry and on the main menu over the dock); `tools\smoke\running-widget.ps1` 16 / 16 PASS (326 = 0x000C0002; the saved file has no dock-level running keys, the running item kept gap 2 / padding 12 and got windows "unstacked" / titles "one" / filter "monitor"); the new **`tools\smoke\quick-settings.ps1`** 6 / 6 PASS (a dock with one clock, showSeconds false / showDate true: QueryState 325 of slot 0 = 2; ItemCommand 866 entry 0 → 1, the saved clock item has showSeconds true and showDate still true; DockCommand 157 → 1, showSeconds false again in the file). After the runs: no WinDock process, `Shell_TrayWnd` visible, the config's SHA-256 the same as before (1BE4C6F1…DE190C46), no test file left in `%LocalAppData%\WinDock`.

**Found and fixed**: nothing (no 13E fix commit).

**Found and left**: none new from the scripts or checks. Still open from the phases: with order "By name" a drag among the running apps snaps back (13D, known); the accessibility client's `WM_GETOBJECT` polling of the foreground WinDock window (12H).

**Release**: the user's dock (12I's exe, started 12:14) quit, `cmake --preset x64-release` + `cmake --build --preset x64-release` + the WinDockTests target, 0 lines with "warning": `build\x64-release\WinDock.exe` **5,769,728 bytes, 2026-11-09 12:18:37** (smaller than 12I's 5,913,088). Their config restored byte for byte before the start; the dock started from the new exe with it: one WinDock process, one dock (QueryState 47 = 1), the Explorer taskbar hidden as `"hideTaskbar": true` wants, the tray takeover on (QueryState 97 = 1; their config has a tray item with `tray.enabled` true, and the `tray-hosted` marker was there before the run too), QueryState 326 = 8 (the theme's gap, padding 0), the Run key unchanged (`build\x64-release`). **The config migrated on that start** (as 13D intends): the file was rewritten (2,575 → 2,555 bytes, SHA-256 C4CB105B…D6BF7B9A); the dock-level `runningFilter`, `windows`, `titles`, `titlesVertical`, `thumbnails`, `thumbnailIntervalS` / `Size` / `Fps` are gone and are now the running item's `settings` (filter "all", windows "stacked", titles "one", titlesVertical "beside", thumbnails "snapshots", 2 / 0 / 10). **Rollback**: the pre-migration file is **`%LocalAppData%\WinDock\config.json.pre-round13.bak`** (SHA-256 1BE4C6F1…DE190C46, taken before any test run): quit the dock, copy it over `config.json`, start the round 12 exe (an older build ignores the running item's settings and would show the defaults).

**Not verified in round 13** (consolidated; all of it is the user's manual pass, the check lists of 13A - 13D above): everything visual and everything by real input. No screenshot was looked at in 13E. Not seen at all: the menu icons and headers per kind, their look in light / dark / high contrast and at another DPI; the dark menus; the quick entries in a real menu (the clock's, the Weather widget's Units submenu, a user widget's `quick` field) and the Undo label; the running apps' gap / padding / order on screen (magnified, dragged, anchored, vertical, ring); the Running apps window and its controls by real input, its menu entry; menus over a dock with auto-hide, a floating / vertical dock, a tray icon's dock menu, real mouse navigation into submenus.

## Round 14: a bar of a fixed size; snapping to the screen's corners and centre (plan: `C:\Users\olson\.claude\plans\windock-round14.md`)
One commit per phase, not pushed. ROADMAP.md "Round 14" has the notes. Round 13's rules: a warning-free `build\dev`, the doc checks, a smoke script where the phase says so, and a manual check list for the user; `build\x64-release` is rebuilt only in 14C.

| Phase | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 14A A bar of a fixed length and thickness | Done, verified by script (dev build, 2026-11-09) | **What**: `docks[].length` (DIPs, 0 = automatic, else 96 - 8000) and `docks[].thickness` (0 = automatic, else 24 - 1000), read / sanitized / written only when set (`Config.h/.cpp`). Layout (`DockWindow::ComputeLayoutAt`): `Layout::fixedLength` (length set, not a ring, not full width): the room is the length (capped by the monitor's room; `maxLength` ignored), the bar is `floor(roomPx)` whether the run fits or overflows, the stage keeps the minimum side slack (`growMax` 0), the run sits in the bar per `align` like a full-width bar's run (without `offset`, which placed the bar), `magnifyBar` steady / always do not apply, and the background does not grow when the run magnifies or a drop gap opens (`MoveItems`: the `fixedBar` cases); an **anchored** fixed-length bar keeps the anchored path (as with `maxLength`; a floating bar's anchors now also apply with `length`); the shrink loop measures a fixed bar by its run. `Layout::thickExtra` = max(0, thickness - automatic): the bar grows by it, the icon row moves `floor(extra / 2)` from the far side, the window `ceil(extra / 2)` (the room for magnified icons unchanged); the widgets' row, the "under" titles (13G), the dots without reflections (they stay under the row) and the separators' hairline follow the row, `barCross` includes it, the slot buttons span the thicker bar. `MaxLengthPx` (QueryState 271) is 0 while a length is set. Settings: Position tab card **Size of the bar** (ids 5860 - 5872: Fixed length / Fixed thickness checks with their boxes and "px", the hint "At least the icons' own height"; checking takes the bar's current size from `SettingsHost::dockRects` and the monitor's DPI, else `maxLength` / 600, 72); collapsed for a ring, Fixed length for a full-width bar, Maximum length hidden (and not counted) while a length is set; Full width's tooltip. Automation: QueryState **328** (`MAKELONG(length, thickness)` px of `layout_.bar`; -2 not a dock, answered by App), DockCommand **184** / **185** (`App::PanelCommand`, one undo entry each). `tools\smoke\WinDock.psm1` gained `Get-DockScale` (`GetDpiForWindow`); new `tools\smoke\fixed-size.ps1`. Docs: README (Position, Configuration, Settings, Automation 184 / 185 / 271 / 328, control ids), guide-settings "Size of the bar", guide-docks. |

| 14B Snap to the screen's corners and centre | Done, verified by script (dev build, 2026-11-09) | **What**: `settings.snapToScreen` (bool, default true, written only when false; `SettingsUiConfig` read / write / `operator==` as `confirmPlacement`). New header `src\SnapLines.h`: `SnapLines::Best(a, b, lines, centres, thr)` (the old per-file `best` lambdas plus the span's centre against the work area's centre lines) and `CentreX` / `CentreY`. Floating bar (`UpdateDockDrag`): the bar's centre also snaps to the work area's two centre lines (`kFloatSnapDip` 8), so corners, the middle of each edge and the screen's centre are reachable. Circle (new): its ring box (`RingScreenRect` minus the drag's shift) snaps to the work area's edges and its centre to the centre lines, then the existing monitor clamp. Standalone widget (`WidgetWindow::Snap`, Grab::Move) and folder window (`FolderWindow::SnapMove`): the centre lines added to the move. Off: none of these snap; the along-edge snap on release (`EndDockDrag`, `kDockSnapDip`: `offset` = the exact distance from the nearest alignment), customize mode's centre detent (`kEditSnapDip`) and the Docks and displays drawing's centre / end snap (`SettingsPagesLayout.cpp`) are off too. Decision beyond the plan: off also turns off the widget's and folder window's **resize** edge snapping (`Snap` with a resize grab, `SnapEdges`), per the plan's decision 4 "no snapping anywhere". Settings: Global > General card **Moving docks** between Full-screen apps and Dashboard (2283 section, 2284 / 2285 check + caption, 2286 hint "Also the edges and other floating docks", the plan's tooltip; `SettingsScope::SaveOnly`). The Position tab's Distance note was left as it is (the extra sentence does not fit its line). Automation: QueryState **329** (App, 0 / 1), DockCommand **186** `kSetSnapToScreen` (App, app-wide; one undo entry "Snap to the screen's corners and centre: On / Off"). Script `tools\smoke\snap-setting.ps1`. **Manual check (14A)**, on a `build\dev` run or after the 14C release:

1. Dock settings > Position > **Size of the bar**: check **Fixed length**; the box shows the bar's length; type 900: the bar is 900 px long with the icons centred in it (Alignment Start / End moves them to an end).
2. Hover the icons: they magnify inside the bar; the bar's ends do not move.
3. Check **Fixed thickness**, type 120: the bar gets thicker, the icons (and dots, titles) stay centred across it; magnified icons still fit above.
4. Turn on Full width: Fixed length disappears; turn it off again. While a fixed length is set, Exact offsets has no Maximum length row.
5. Uncheck both: the bar fits its icons again; the dock menu's Undo names the change.

**Manual check (14B)**, on a `build\dev` run or after the 14C release:

1. Drag a floating dock slowly toward the screen's centre and each corner: it settles there (both centre lines / two edges); along an edge it stops at the edge's middle.
2. Drag a circle, a standalone widget and a folder window the same way: each snaps to the edges, the middle of the edges and the centre.
3. App settings > General > Moving docks: clear **Snap to the screen's corners and centre**; repeat 1 - 2: nothing snaps (also not at the edges or other docks), and a widget's resize does not snap either.
4. Still off: Ctrl+drag a bottom dock a little off centre and release: it stays there (Position > Distance shows the small offset); customize mode's drag has no centre pause.
5. Turn it back on; the dock menu's Undo names the change.

**Manual check (14D)**, on the release exe:

1. On a right-edge dock with snapshot thumbnails, Running apps settings > Tile length 100, Tile thickness 180: the tiles are landscape and the bar grows to fit them.
2. The pinned icons sit centred across the thicker bar; hovering a tile magnifies it toward the screen's middle, fully visible, with the label beyond it.
3. Close every window that has a tile: the bar becomes thin again; open one: it grows back.
4. Turn titles on (under / beside): they sit under the tiles, not over them.
5. Tile thickness 0: tiles are an icon thick again and the key is gone from config.json.

### Round 14 final regression (2026-11-09)

Phase 14C, on 9b3135b (14B). Light by design (the plan's "How this round is run"): static checks, every smoke script once, the release build. No subagents, no screenshots, no real input.

**Static checks**: `build\dev` (14B's build) warning-free; `python tools\check_docs.py` clean (81 help topics); `python tools\check_widget_docs.py` no drift; `build\dev\WinDockTests.exe` 3 case files, 0 failures; `build\dev\WinDock.exe --validate-widget examples\minimal` exit 0 "OK: no problems".

**Smoke scripts** on `build\dev\WinDock.exe`, one after the other with `-NoRestart` (the user's dock quit before the first; each backs up and restores the config byte for byte): `fixed-size.ps1` 6 / 6 PASS (328 = 600 x 120 px at scale 1.0; 184 800 → `"length": 800`; 184 0 → the key gone, bar 72 px; 185 0 → `thickness` gone, bar 64 px; 328 of dock 9 = -2), `snap-setting.ps1` 3 / 3 PASS (329 = 1; 186 0 → `"snapToScreen": false`; 186 1 → the key gone), `menu-zorder.ps1` 12 / 12 PASS, `running-widget.ps1` 16 / 16 PASS, `quick-settings.ps1` 6 / 6 PASS, `quick-setup.ps1` 14 / 14 PASS. After the runs: no WinDock process, the config's SHA-256 the same as before (438747632642E4BF…EEB72C886D).

**Found and fixed**: nothing (no 14C fix commit).

**Found and left**: none new. From the phases: nothing visual was seen (the "Not verified" lists of 14A / 14B). Note for the next release: `run.bat` must be called by its full path from Claude's PowerShell tool (`& cmd /c E:\repos\windows-dock\run.bat`; a bare `run.bat` is "not recognized"), and its `start` of the dock keeps the tool's pipe open, so the call does not return until the dock quits: run it in the background or use `--norun` and start the dock separately.

**Release**: `run.bat` (`cmake --preset x64-release` + build), 0 warnings: `build\x64-release\WinDock.exe` **5,803,520 bytes, 2026-11-09 14:57:50**. The dock started from it with the user's config (unchanged by this round: no migration): one WinDock process, one dock (QueryState 47 = 1), QueryState 328 = 0x004005A0 (the bar 1440 x 64 px, automatic size), 329 = 1 (snapping on), the Explorer taskbar hidden as `"hideTaskbar": true` wants.

**Not verified in round 14** (consolidated; the user's manual pass, the check lists of 14A / 14B above): everything visual and everything by real input. Not seen at all: a fixed-length or fixed-thickness bar on screen (icons centred across a thick bar, a magnified run inside a fixed bar, drop gaps, scrolling, anchored / floating / vertical fixed bars, auto-hide and the app bar band of a thick bar, customize mode on a fixed bar), the Size of the bar card and the Moving docks card by real input, any real drag's snapping (floating bar, circle, standalone widget, folder window; corners, edge middles, the centre; with the setting off), another DPI, a second display.

## Round 16: the Start menu opens beside the Start button (plan: `C:\Users\olson\.claude\plans\windock-round16.md`)
One commit per phase, not pushed. ROADMAP.md "Round 16" has the measurements, the design and the hazards. Round 13's rules: a warning-free `build\dev`, the doc checks, the smoke script, a manual check list for the user, `run.bat` at the end.

| Phase | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 16A `StartMenuPlacer`, the Start widget's Menu position, `sendKeys('start-near')` | Done, verified by script (dev and release builds, 2026-11-09) | From the user's request "make it so the start menu appears near wherever the user actually clicked the start button widget". **Probe** (recorded in `StartMenuPlacer.cpp`): UI Automation gives the host's whole tree while it is cloaked but **every rectangle is empty** until the menu is open (re-checked by this phase's second agent: all seven raw-view children `Empty` with the host cloaked = 2 at `0,0 - 5120,1392`); there is **no single panel element**: the host's raw-view children are the panel's parts (`SearchBoxToggleButton`, `ShowHideCompanion`, `TopLevelRoot` SemanticZoom, `UserControl`, the Phone Link pane's `NamedContainerAutomationPeer`, `ActionBarOverflowButton`, `CloseStartAccessibleButton` always empty), and open their union is the panel's content (2003,531 - 3117,1367, centred on the host, 25 px above its bottom). `TaskbarAl` 0 was not tried. **Adapted from the plan**: `PanelRect` cannot know the panel before the first open, so the panel is read (the union of the children plus an estimated 12 DIP frame) by the watch 500 ms after the menu was seen open beside a button, kept as an offset inside the host, and used from the next click on; before that the anchor model's guess (1138 x 860 DIPs, its bottom 13 DIPs above the host's bottom, centred; the plan's 640 x 650 was too small for the measured content). A button on a monitor whose DPI differs from the host's gets Windows' place (moving the full-screen host across a DPI boundary is not measured). **What**: `src\StartMenuPlacer.h/.cpp` (owned by `App`; `Host` / `IsOpen` / `PanelRect` / `Place(box, side, monitor, dryRun)` / `Restore` / the watch / `EmergencyRestore`), the watch a 250 ms one-shot timer 45 on the app window only while shifted (restores when the host is gone or cloaked again after being seen open, or never seen open in 3 s); restores also in `App::Shutdown`, `WM_QUERYENDSESSION`, on a refused `SendInput`, after a dry run, and from `CrashRestoreTaskbar`. Script API: allow-list `start-near` (`WidgetInstance::SendKeys`; box / side from `Client::WidgetEditorPlace`, the placer via the new `Client::WidgetStartPlacer()` → `DockShared::StartPlacer()`; dry-run line `start-near\t5B\t<dx> <dy>`). Start widget 1.2.0: `menuPosition` `near` (default) / `windows`. QueryState **331** / **332** (App). Module: `Start-WinDock -Environment`, `Get-StartHostWindow` (FindWindowEx: `EnumWindows` does not list CoreWindows). Script `tools\smoke\start-near.ps1`. Docs: README (Start widget, Automation 331 / 332), docs/widgets.md (allow-list row, `widgets/start`), guide-items, troubleshooting, quick-start. |

**Manual check (16A)**, on the release exe:

1. Click the Start widget on a bottom dock: the menu opens over the button, its bottom just above the bar; press Escape. A second click: the same place (now from the measured panel).
2. Press the Windows key: the menu is at its usual place.
3. Click the widget, then click the desktop (the menu closes); press the Windows key: the usual place.
4. With Start open from the widget, run `WinDock.exe --quit`: the next Windows key opens it at the usual place (restart the dock after).
5. The widget's settings > Content > Start menu > Menu position "Where Windows puts it": a click opens it at the usual place. A left / top dock if you have one.

| 16E A widget dragged off the dock becomes a standalone widget | Done, built into `build\dev`, not committed, not run (2026-11-09) | From the user's request: "when you drag a widget off a dock it should become a standalone desktop widget (unless it's something like the running apps widget which needs to be on a dock)". **What**: `DockWindow::BeginDrag` decides once per drag (`dragToDesktop_`) whether the dragged slot is a script widget (config item `ItemKind::Widget`) whose manifest allows the desktop (`Widgets::CanBeStandalone`), on an unlocked dock; `UpdateDrag` dims it in the remove zone as a pin (`kRemoveOpacity`); `EndDrag` there does not fade / unpin: it lands back like a cancelled drag, then calls the new `PinRequests::widgetToDesktop(item, cursor)`. `App::MoveWidgetToDesktop` refuses (beep) a locked dock, anything but a script widget (Clock / Weather / Tray / Notifications kinds, running apps never get here) and a dock-only widget; else it copies the entry, erases it from the dock (`ReloadPins`), and makes the standalone entry from the copy (new overload `AddWidgetWindow(DockItemEntry, nearDock, open)`, centred on the point through `widgetAt_`; `anchor` / `offset` dropped; on an unexpected refusal the item is put back). One NoteLabel "Moved <name> to the desktop" (snapshot undo: the dock menu's Undo puts it back). New manifest key `standalone.supported` (default true; parsed and validated in `WidgetPackage.cpp`, no built-in sets it); `AddWidgetWindow` (so Add to the desktop, Docks and displays' Add) and the drawer's desktop drop (`App::DrawerDrag`, its answer cached per drag) refuse such a widget. ItemCommand 867 `kWidgetToDesktop` (the work-area centre of the dock window's monitor). Docs: README (item menu, Standalone widgets, Automation 867), `docs\widgets.md` (manifest table, Standalone widgets), `docs\guide-items.md`. Smoke script `tools\smoke\widget-to-desktop.ps1` (written, not run). Correction to the plan: `storage` is per widget id (`WidgetRuntime`), not per dock item, so the moved widget sees the same store; only its running state (init again) is new. |
| | | **Verified**: `build\dev` warning-free (WinDock); `check_docs.py` clean (81 help topics), `check_widget_docs.py` no drift; the smoke script parses; `build\dev\WinDock.exe --validate-widget` on a copy of `examples\minimal` with `"standalone": { "supported": false }`: OK, and with `"supported": "no"`: the error "standalone.supported: is a string, not a boolean" (exit 1). |
| | | **Not verified**: the dock was not run: the drag on screen (the dim in the zone, the landing, the window under the pointer, the dock's width snapping after the rebuild), customize mode's drag into the zone, the smoke script, a dock-only widget actually refused by the drag / drawer / Add to the desktop, Undo after the move. `build\x64-release` not rebuilt. |

**Manual check (16E)**, on a `build\dev` run or the release exe:

1. Drag a Hello or Digital clock widget up off a bottom dock: past the remove zone it dims; release: it leaves the dock and appears on the desktop centred under the pointer, with its settings kept (e.g. Digital clock's Show seconds).
2. The same drag, press Escape before releasing: it goes back to its slot, nothing changes. Release back on the bar: it only reorders, as before.
3. Lock the dock: the widget cannot be dragged at all (as before); ItemCommand 867 returns 0.
4. Drag the native Clock, Weather, Tray or Notification centre slot up into the zone: no dimming, it snaps back. A pinned app that is not running is still removed with the fade; a running app is not.
5. After a move, the dock menu's Undo ("Moved Digital clock to the desktop") puts it back on the dock and removes the window (the timeline is a config snapshot, so one step undoes both).
6. In customize mode: a drag into the zone converts it the same way; a drop on an anchor target still sets the anchor.

| 16F Animated Meteocons through composition layers | Done, built into `build\dev`, not committed, not run (2026-11-10) | From the user's "suppose i want you to make it so Meteocons works animated" / "enable gpu rendering ... i dont want you to write a whole engine" / "just make it work". **What**: (a) the widget API's **composition layers**: `canvas.layer(id, x, y, w, h, { parent, clip, opacity, tracks })` / `canvas.endLayer()` (`WidgetCanvas::BeginLayer` / `EndLayer` with a `LayerSink`; `QuickJsEngine.cpp` `LayerFn` / `ReadTrack`): on a dock slot or a standalone widget `WidgetInstance` (the sink) records the layer's content into a D2D command list on the renderer's resource context during `draw()` and, after the slot's own `EndDraw`, draws it into a child visual of the slot (`RealizeLayers`), places / clips it and attaches DirectComposition animations built from the tracks (`BuildTrackAnimation`: Hermite cubic pieces per key span, the `begin` phase shift with a cut at the wrap, `AddRepeat`; rotate about a centre + translate as a transform group, opacity on `IDCompositionVisual3`); unchanged options keep the loops running, undeclared layers are destroyed, a recreated parent visual is noticed by pointer; popups, previews, `measure()` and reduce motion draw inline at the first keys. (b) `tools/meteocons_convert.py` + `widgets/weather/meteocons.js` (IDR 359, `app.rc`, `WidgetPackage.cpp`): 20 Meteocons fill icons (MIT, @meteocons/svg 0.1.0 via unpkg; 78 parts, 105 tracks, 59 KB). (c) The widget: `iconPack` `meteocons`, `iconMotion` (quick), `drawMeteocon` (a clipped group per icon with the stale dimming, a layer per part, the fog's masked sun as a clipped sub-group), the pin / refresh arrow from Lucide. Every caller goes through `RequestWidgetToDock`, which checks, queues the move by ids in `pendingDockMoves_` and posts `kMoveToDockMessage` (WM_APP + 72, app window; listed in DockWindow.h's table): both gestures run inside the widget window's own messages and the move destroys it. **Manual check (16G)**, on a `build\dev` run or the release exe:

1. Drag a standalone Digital clock (Show seconds on) onto the dock's bar: the insertion marker opens between the items as the pointer moves along the bar; release: it joins the dock there, the window goes, Show seconds is still on.
2. Release it off the bar (on the desktop, or on the dock's window but outside the bar): it just moves, as before; the marker is gone.
3. Start a drag over the bar, press Escape before releasing: the window goes back where it was, the marker goes. Also for a resize drag.
4. Lock the dock: dragging the widget over it shows no marker and a release only moves the window; right-click > Move to dock lists the dock greyed "(locked)".
5. Right-click the widget > Move to dock > the dock: appended at the end. With only folder windows / widgets besides a turned-off dock, the submenu is greyed.
6. The dock menu's Undo ("Moved Digital clock to …") brings the window back where it was and takes the slot off the dock.
7. Optionally `pwsh -File tools\smoke\widget-to-dock.ps1` (quits the user's dock and restarts it).

| 17A The Photo frame widget; `file` / `folder` setting fields; `canvas.image` of any file | Done, verified by script, committed dafead3 (2026-11-11) | From the user's request: "make a new photo frame widget - can load a single image (png, jpg, webp, jpeg, at least) or user selects a folder of images for a slideshow. user can define width/height of the photo or select predefined sizes/aspect-ratios, users can force aspect ratios on slideshows and set to cover (center center) or contain ..., basic border options, drop shadow options, ... borderless w/ drop shadows, ... order (alphabetically/numerically descending or ascending, oldest to newest, newest to oldest, or random), time between slides and transition options of fade in/out, appear, slide from (directions)". **Native**: (1) `SettingField::Type::File` / `Folder` (`WidgetPackage.h/.cpp`: `TypeOf`, the string default, the merge, `kTypeNames`, the new schema key `filter` in `kFieldKeys`, warned on a non-file field); the generated Item page (`SettingsPagesWidgets.cpp`) shows the path box (`3700 + 10 k + 1`, 1024 chars) with a **Browse…** button (part 7, `kWidgetFieldBrowse`, `SettingsIds.h`) that opens `IFileOpenDialog` (`PickPath`: `FOS_PICKFOLDERS` for a folder, the `filter` patterns for a file, started in the current value's folder) and stores the choice; the Contents inspector and the settings search treat both as strings (their `default` branches). (2) `canvas.image` / `imageSize` take an absolute path (`X:\`, `\\`, `%VAR%`) with the `shell` permission: `AssetPath` (QuickJsEngine.cpp) makes a `"file:"` token (a module name never carries one: `ResolvePath` refuses `:` and `\`), `WidgetInstance::ReadFile` reads the file (64 MB cap, `%VAR%` expanded), and `WidgetCanvas::FindImage` decodes it photo-sized: WIC's scaler to at most 2048 px on the long side (the JPEG decoder scales while decoding), the EXIF orientation (tag 274 under `/app1/ifd` or `/ifd`) applied as a flip then a rotation (two `IWICBitmapFlipRotator`s: the order of combined flags is not documented and the transposed orientations 5 / 7 depend on it), raw sizes over 24000 px refused; decoded photos count against a 64 MB budget per canvas, the least recently used evicted first (`ImageEntry::file / bytes / stamp`, `fileBytes_`); package images keep the old 8 MB / 4096 px / 32-entry rule. **Widget** `widgets\photo-frame` (`IDR_WIDGET_PHOTOFRAME_JSON` / `_MAIN` 368 / 369, `app.rc`, `WidgetPackage.cpp`; `timeLimitMs` 1000 for the decode inside a draw): Content: `source` image / folder, `image` (file, an image filter), `folder` (folder), `order` name-asc (Explorer's natural order from `shell.list`) / name-desc / oldest / newest / random (a shuffle per round, re-listed at each wrap), `interval` 1 - 3600 s (one `setTimeout`; none while hidden or, with `pauseOnHover`, hovered), `transition` none / fade / slide-left / -right / -top / -bottom + `transitionMs` (the script's own 16 ms `setInterval` for that long only; never with Reduce motion or in a preview; a crossfade, or the old picture sliding out as the new one slides in), `click` next / open / folder / none, `wheel`; Appearance: `size` icon / small 64 / medium 96 / large 160 / custom `width`, `aspect` image (the picture's own, via `canvas.imageSize` in `measure()`) / 1:1 / 4:3 / 3:2 / 16:9 / 3:4 / 2:3 / 9:16 / custom `height` (standalone: the largest picture of that shape inside the window), `fit` cover / contain + `background`, `border` none / line / mat / polaroid + `borderWidth` / `borderColor` / `cornerRadius`, `shadow` none / soft / medium / strong (eight translucent rounded rectangles growing outward) + `shadowOffset`. The next picture is decoded at the end of `draw()`. Quick menu: source, order, transition, aspect, fit; common: source, image, folder, interval, size, fit. Preview: a drawn sample landscape. Test hook `debugStore` (`shown:` / `state:` + `dock.itemKey`). Docs: docs/widgets.md (built-in list, the schema table's `file` / `folder` rows and `filter`, the `image` / `imageSize` rows, `widgets/photo-frame`), README (the widget list, widget.json's types, the Item window's controls), guide-items. Module: `Get-ChildControl` / `Get-ControlText` (EnumChildWindows by control id). Script `tools\smoke\photo-frame.ps1`. |
| | | **Verified** (2026-11-11): `build\dev` warning-free (WinDock); `check_docs.py` clean (81 help topics), `check_widget_docs.py` no drift; `--validate-widget widgets\photo-frame` OK, no warnings; `photo-frame.ps1` on `build\dev`: all checks pass (five generated pictures, the text file excluded; the slideshow at 1 s showed `1.png, 2.jpg, 10.png, portrait.jpg, wide.jpg, 1.png` in that order; the dock frame 4:3; the standalone frame 3:4 from the portrait picture, inside its window; QueryState 244 = 0, 58 counting), the user's config restored and their dock restarted. The two PNGs the script saves were looked at: the dock shows the red 4:3 picture in a white mat with a soft shadow beside the clock; the standalone one a Polaroid (wider bottom band) with a strong shadow and the portrait picture cropped to fill. |
| | | **Not verified**: real photos (a JPEG with an EXIF orientation other than 1: the flip-then-rotate mapping was derived, not seen), WebP / HEIC / AVIF (they need Windows' codecs: WebP ships with Windows 11, HEIC needs the HEVC extension), a 20 MP photo's decode time inside a draw (synchronous: a 100 - 200 ms stall per picture change is expected; a worker-thread decode is the follow-up if it shows), the slide transitions on screen, Reduce motion, a left / right dock, the Browse… button's dialog itself (modal: the script only checks it exists), the Contents inspector's box for `image` / `folder`, the quick menu entries, a folder of 1000+ files (`shell.list` stops at 1000), a folder that changes while the slideshow runs (picked up at the next round), `build\x64-release` not rebuilt. Known limits: no sub-folders; no file drop onto the frame (a widget cannot write its own settings; `onDrop` is left out); the shadow is a stack, not a blur; a `cornerRadius` larger than the border makes the picture's corners round too (concentric). |

**Manual check (17A)**, on a `build\dev` run or the release exe:

1. Dock settings > Contents > add Photo frame; in the inspector choose One picture, Browse… to a photo of yours (a phone photo taken in portrait, ideally): it shows upright, cropped to 4:3 in a white mat with a soft shadow; Fits inside the frame shows bands instead.
2. Widget settings > Appearance: Size Large, Aspect ratio "The picture's own": the frame takes the photo's shape; Border None + Drop shadow Strong: the borderless print; Polaroid; Rounded corners 12.
3. Content > A folder of pictures: Browse… to a folder; Order Random, Time per picture 3, Transition Fade, then each Slide: the pictures change with a crossfade / a slide; with App settings > Animation > Reduce motion they just appear.
4. The wheel over the frame steps through the pictures; a click shows the next one; a middle click opens the picture; "Pause while the pointer is over it" holds it under the pointer.
5. Right-click the frame: Show / Order / Transition / Aspect ratio / A picture of another shape are in the menu. Add to the desktop: resize the window; the frame keeps the aspect inside it.
6. A left dock: the frame stands upright (the same picture, the same shape); Icon size follows the dock's icon size.

| 18D The settings search opens at once: the index is built after the dialog shows, one window per posted message; the index's hidden windows are kept while a settings window is open; the time zone list is cached | Done, verified by script (2026-11-11) | From the user: "when I use the search feature in the settings it lags like hell before eventually loading the dialog". **The cause**: `SettingsSearch::Create` built the whole index before `ShowWindow`, and the index needs a settings window per docks[] entry (the Global window, then one Dock window each: a dock, a standalone widget, a folder window), each created hidden with every page and its hundreds of controls (`App::CreateHiddenSettings`); with the user's eleven entries (one dock, nine standalone widgets, five of them clocks whose Content page enumerates the ~140 Windows time zones from the registry) that was seconds of a frozen UI thread before anything appeared, and the dialog's own activation then rebuilt the index a second time; the hidden windows were destroyed with the last search dialog, so every Ctrl+F paid it again. **Now** (`SettingsSearch.cpp`): `Rebuild` only starts the build (`indexing_`; `kIndexTimerId` 1, a one-shot 10 ms timer on the dialog's window: a chain of posted messages, the first try, kept the dialog white and deaf until the end, since Windows dispatches posted messages before input and paint, and a timer fires only once those are done); `IndexStep` reads one window per tick (`SettingsSearchHost::windowCount` / `window(i)`: 0 the Global window, 1 + k docks[k]'s, created hidden when not open), so the dialog paints and takes keys between two windows; the first build publishes the rows read so far after every step (`indexPartial_`: the list fills while the user types, a row they selected stays selected), a later rebuild keeps the old rows until it is done; `FinishIndex` adds the items' fields (`IndexItems`) and restores the selection by key. The status line says "Indexing settings… 3 of 12 windows" (with "N settings so far" / "x of N so far" once there are rows) until it is done. `WM_ACTIVATE` no longer posts a rebuild while a build runs (the double build on open). `Change` finds a row's window by its number (`Row::window`). **Measured** (`GetTickCount64` laps, since removed, and a plain PowerShell process creating 300 children of a hidden popup): on this machine a child window costs ~0.5 ms to create and 3 - 5 ms to destroy, for any class in any process, so the cost is the control count: a Dock window (Contents ~100, Position ~90, Look ~290, Behaviour ~60 controls) is ~1.2 s to make (controls 0.5 s, theme 0.27, layout 0.27, the Contents tab's Load 0.5: the widget catalog and every icon) and ~2.5 s to destroy; a clock widget's window (three tabs, the time zone combo filled with ~140 rows) ~0.5 s each way; the App settings window ~1.3 s / 2.4 s. **A window made for the index** (`SettingsWindow::indexOnly_`, `Create(show = false)`) therefore skips what the index never reads: no tooltips, the Contents tab (not indexed) is an empty page window, and `Refresh` only marks it stale (`indexStale_`; `EnsureFresh`, called by the index before it reads, loads the indexed pages then), so a dozen hidden windows cost nothing on every change made in the open settings window (until 18D each change reloaded them all, the Contents tab's icons included); `Activate` (Go to, or an Open* path finding it) builds the Contents tab (`RebuildPage`), makes the tooltips and refreshes before it shows. **App**: the hidden windows stay while a search dialog or a settings window of the user's is open (the next Ctrl+F is instant; until 18D they went with the last dialog, which froze the dock for their destruction right after Esc) and go when the last of those closes, one per posted `kDropSearchWindow` (WM_APP + 73; `DropSearchWindows` / `DropOneSearchWindow`, from `kSearchClosed` and `kSettingsClosed`; a search or window opened meanwhile keeps the rest), so the dock takes messages between two of them. At the app's shutdown the hidden index windows are abandoned, not destroyed (`SettingsWindow::AbandonForExit`: the window procedures detached, the HWNDs die with the process): the user's own dock, with ten of them alive, took over ten seconds to quit (`Stop-WinDock` timed out at 10 s) with the taskbar still hidden meanwhile. `Clock::TimeZones` caches its list for ten minutes (every clock widget's page and the index enumerated it). **The user's first look** (their own config, the release build): "when I start searching it appears white until I type": the dialog's controls stayed unpainted during the build, because the steps chained through posted messages and Windows dispatches posted messages before input and paint; the steps now run from a one-shot 10 ms timer (`kIndexTimerId`), which fires only once input and paint are done, and `UpdateWindow` paints the dialog once before the first step. Automation: QueryState **338** (the dialog's index is complete 1 / being built 0 / -2 none), **339** (hidden index windows; LOWORD 1: live dialog objects). Docs: README (Find a setting, the ids, QueryState 338 / 339), guide-settings (Find a setting). Script: `tools\smoke\settings-search.ps1`. |
| | | **Verified** (2026-11-11): `build\dev` warning-free (WinDock); `check_docs.py` clean (82 help topics), `check_widget_docs.py` no drift; `tools\smoke\settings-search.ps1` on `build\dev` (one dock, five analog clocks and a Notes widget standalone): all 29 checks pass: the dialog is visible 145 - 222 ms after DockCommand 151 (338 = 0 then), the index of 266 rows complete ~2.2 s later (338 = 1; it was ~2.6 s before the index-only windows), the status "266 settings", seven hidden `WinDock.Settings` windows; "clock" 98 rows, "zzzz" none, cleared 266; Go to on a "dock smoke behaviour" row shows the Dock window (223 = 1) with its Contents tab built late (278 = 6 built-in drawer rows), the dialog stays and re-indexes; Esc keeps the six remaining hidden windows (339 = 6) and frees the dialog object (339 / 1 = 0); 151 again: the dialog in ~170 ms with the index complete within 1.5 s; WM_CLOSE to the two visible windows: every settings window gone in ~7.3 s (one per posted message), 339 = 0. The PNG was looked at: the dark dialog, three columns, the first rows "Start with Windows … App › General › System … On", "266 settings", Go to. Before the stepped index (the first run of the script) the same config took ~2.6 s between the command and the dialog with nothing on screen. After the timer change (all 34 checks pass again): a second PNG taken ~100 ms after the dialog showed, while the index was being built, is fully painted (dark list with 176 rows, the box, Go to, "176 settings so far · Indexing settings… 3 of 8 windows"), where the user's screenshot of the posted-message build showed the list, box and button white; with the hidden windows alive, `--quit` ends the process in 6.6 - 7.9 s as `Stop-WinDock` measures it (its own waits included; ~2.5 s with no settings window), under the 10 s it timed out at before. The user's config was restored byte for byte, no WinDock process left, `Shell_TrayWnd` visible before the user's dock was restarted. |
| | | **Not verified**: the user's own config (eleven entries: one dock of 14 items, a two-item dock, nine standalone widgets; the index should take 6 - 8 s in steps with the dialog usable throughout, and closing the last settings window should cost a hitch of up to ~2.5 s per dock window and ~0.5 s per widget window, spread), typing while the list fills (the partial publish keeps a selected row; only the complete index was driven), a rebuild while one is running (a Go to during the first build), Space / a drop-down change in place on a hidden window's row (`SearchChange` on a window without tooltips: unchanged code), the Contents tab built by `RebuildPage` when Go to shows a hidden Dock window of a dock with many items (six drawer rows were seen, the strip not driven), a weather / notifications / calendar change reaching an index-only window (their handlers run on its controls as before), the light theme, `build\x64-release` rebuilt by `run.bat --norun`. **Manual check (18D)**, on the release exe after `run.bat`:

1. Open App settings, press Ctrl+F: the dialog appears immediately with "Indexing settings… k of n windows" under the list, the list fills over the next second or two, then the line says "N settings". Type while it fills: the list filters what is there.
2. Press Esc, press Ctrl+F again: the full list is there at once.
3. Close every settings window, open one and press Ctrl+F: it indexes again (the hidden windows went with the last window).
4. Go to a result in a standalone widget's panel: the panel opens beside the widget as before.

**Manual check (18C)**, on the release exe after `run.bat`:

1. App settings > Docks and displays: the Add a dock card ends with Floating dock, Folder window…, Standalone widget… (each with a small picture); the list's buttons have pictures too; greyed ones look greyed. Switch Windows to light mode: the pictures follow the text colour.
2. Click Standalone widget…: the picker opens under that button. Click Floating dock: a floating dock appears.
3. With App settings open, look at your dock's running apps: a WinDock slot is there. Cover the window with others, click the slot: it comes back. Hover the slot: its preview shows the window; close it from there.
4. Close App settings: the slot goes.

**Manual check (18B)**, on the release exe after `run.bat`:

1. App settings > Docks and displays > Add a dock > Standalone widget… (More ▾ > Standalone widget… before 18C): the picker opens under the button with every widget's preview; it looks right in your theme.
2. Click a widget: it appears centred on the primary display with its panel beside it, the picker is gone, the list selects the new entry.
3. Open it again, drag a tile onto the desktop: the widget lands where you let go; drag one onto the dock: the dock's marker shows and it joins the dock.
4. Type in the search box, press Esc: the picker closes; click elsewhere while it is open: it closes too.

**Manual check (18A)**, on the release exe after `run.bat`:

1. App settings > Docks and displays: the Add a dock tiles look right in the dark theme and the light one (Settings > Personalization > Colors).
2. Move the pointer over the tiles: each draws a dashed outline on the drawing above and its description under the tiles; leaving the tiles clears both.
3. Click Modern, then Windows 7: the live dock changes from one to the other; Keep keeps Windows 7 (one Undo entry).
4. Select a dock, Save as layout…: its tile appears under Mine.
5. Folder window… (More ▾ > Folder window… before 18C) still adds a folder window.
